# List of Free-Text Explanations Required or Requested by the IRS

This table lists all instances that could be found where the IRS requires or requests  free-form explanations, narratives, statements, and the like.  Pure computations, fixed declarations, copies of existing documents, and purely structured lists are outside the scope. Substantively identical directions are presented only once; for example, an explanation requested by both a tax form and a Treasury regulation is listed just once, with the additional source listed in the column "Also at".  Only English-language versions are included to avoid double-counting.  Created with the help of OpenAI Codex and Claude Cowork.  


**Total: 1314.** Mandatory: 1262. Optional: 52.

* Mandatory Free-Text Attachments: 699
* Optional Free-Text Attachments: 38
* Mandatory Free-Text in Form, Attachment if Long: 537
* Optional Free-Text in Form, Attachment if Long: 14
* Mandatory Free-Text in Form, Attachment Not Contemplated: 26
* Optional Free-Text in Form, Attachment Not Contemplated: 0

*Mandatory* = the source says must, shall, attach, should, explain, etc. (including conditional requirements); *Optional* = the source expressly makes the statement discretionary (may, can, optional, encouraged). *Free-Text Attachments* = the explanation goes only in a separate statement, attachment, letter, or written submission; *Attachment if Long* = there is space on a form (a line, box, or supplemental part such as Schedule O, or an online text field), and the form, its instructions, or the return it is filed with contemplate additional sheets, continuation copies, accompanying statements (for example, a signature declaration covering “accompanying schedules and statements”), or an uploaded supplement; *Attachment Not Contemplated* = there is space on the form and nothing in the form, its instructions, or the return it is filed with provides for going beyond it, or the form sets an express length limit (for example, 250 characters). The table is grouped in that order, mandatory groups first and optional groups at the bottom, each group in its own table under a heading.

| [Instructions for Form 1120-F, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i1120f.pdf) | Instructions for Form 1120-F, Refunds of U.S. withholding. | [Instructions for Form 1120-F, at 10](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i1120f.pdf) | Instructions for Form 1120-F, Penalty for late filing of return. | [Instructions for Form 1120-F, at 37](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i1120f.pdf) | Instructions for Form 1120-F, worksheet for Schedule W, line 3 (overpayment under section 6611(e)(4)), line b. | [Publication 225, at 11](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p225.pdf) | Publication 225, chapter 3 (weather-related sales of livestock). You must explain any difference in a schedule attached to your return. |  |
| [Publication 463, at 13](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p463.pdf) | Publication 463, Conventions held on cruise ships. To deduct expenses of a business meeting held on a U.S.-registered cruise ship, the taxpayer attaches a signed written statement describing the total days of the trip, the hours each day devoted to scheduled business activities, and the program of scheduled business activities. | 4. You attach to your return a written statement signed by you that includes information about: a. The total days of the trip (not including the days of transportation to and from the cruise ship port), b. The number of hours each day that you devoted to scheduled business activities, and c. A program of the scheduled business activities of the meeting. |  |
| [Publication 501, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p501.pdf) | Publication 501, Signing a joint return, spouse in combat zone. A taxpayer who signs a joint return for a spouse who cannot sign because the spouse is serving in a combat zone attaches a signed statement explaining that the spouse is serving in a combat zone. | Attach a signed statement to your return explaining that your spouse is serving in a combat zone. |  |
| [Publication 503, at 9](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p503.pdf) | Publication 503, Provider refusal. If a care provider refuses to give identifying information needed for Form 2441, the taxpayer enters “See Attached Statement” and attaches a statement explaining that the information was requested but the provider did not give it, to show due diligence. | Enter “See Attached Statement” in the columns calling for the information you don't have. Then attach a statement explaining that you requested the information from the care provider, but the provider didn't give you the information. |  |
| [Publication 510, at 31](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p510.pdf) | Publication 510, Chapter 5, Manufacturers Taxes, Credits or Refunds. A manufacturer claiming a credit or refund of manufacturers excise tax for certain uses, sales, exports, or price readjustments must set forth in detail the facts on which the claim is based. | The manufacturer may be eligible to obtain a credit or refund of the manufacturers tax for certain uses, sales, exports, and price readjustments. The claim must set forth in detail the facts upon which the claim is based. |  |
| [Publication 514, at 24](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p514.pdf) | Publication 514, Alternative basis for sourcing compensation. An employee with total compensation of $250,000 or more who sources compensation under an alternative basis checks the box on Form 1116, line 1b, and attaches a statement identifying the compensation, the alternative basis used, how the allocation was computed, and a comparison with the time or geographical basis. | Also, if your total compensation from all sources was $250,000 or more, you must check the box on Form 1116, line 1b, and attach a written statement to your tax return that sets forth all of the following. 1. Your name and social security number (written across the top of the statement). 2. The specific compensation income, or the specific fringe benefit, for which you are using the alternative basis. 3. For each item in (2), the alternative basis of allocation of source used. 4. For each item in (2), a computation showing how the alternative allocation was computed. 5. A comparison of the dollar amount of the U.S. compensation and foreign compensation sourced under both the alternative basis and the time or geographical basis discussed earlier. | <sub>Publication 519, at 19</sub> |
| [Publication 515, at 77](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p515.pdf) | Publication 515, Category (6) nonstandard applications for a withholding certificate (section 1445). An application of a type not otherwise described must explain in detail the proposed basis for issuing the certificate and the reasons justifying issuance on that basis. | An application for a withholding certificate not previously described must explain in detail the proposed basis for the issuance of the certificate and set forth the reasons justifying the issuance of a certificate on that basis. |  |
| [Publication 517, at 12](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p517.pdf) | Publication 517, ministers without an approved Form 4361 exemption. A duly ordained minister employed by a church who owes SE tax on ministerial wages reports those wages, less allowable expenses, on Schedule SE (Form 1040), line 2, and attaches an explanation. | However, if you were a duly ordained minister who was an employee of a church and you must pay SE tax on the wages you earned for those services, don't report those wages on Schedule C (Form 1040). Instead, report those wages less any allowable expenses on line 2 of Schedule SE (Form 1040) and attach an explanation. |  |
| [Publication 519, at 57](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p519.pdf) | Publication 519, Refund of Taxes Withheld in Error, Multilevel marketing. A distributor seeking a refund of tax withheld in error attaches to the return or refund claim supporting information, including a statement explaining why the income reported on Form W-2, 1042-S, or 1099 is not subject to U.S. tax. | You must also attach to the U.S. income tax return or claim for refund supporting information that includes, but is not limited to, the following items. • A copy of your Form W-2, Form 1042-S, or Form 1099 to prove the amount of taxes withheld. • A statement explaining why income reported on your Form W-2, Form 1042-S, or Form 1099 is not subject to U.S. taxation. |  |
| [Publication 531, at 3](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p531.pdf) | Publication 531, Penalty for not reporting tips. An employee who failed to report tips to the employer and claims reasonable cause to avoid the 50% penalty attaches a statement to the return explaining why the tips were not reported. | You can avoid this penalty if you can show reasonable cause for not reporting the tips to your employer. To do so, attach a statement to your return explaining why you didn’t report them. |  |
| [Publication 544, at 16](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p544.pdf) | Publication 544, Changing your mind (condemnations). A taxpayer who reported the entire condemnation gain and later buys replacement property and wants to postpone part of the gain files an amended return with a statement explaining that the entire gain was previously reported. | You should file a claim for refund on Form 1040-X (or other applicable amended return). Include a statement explaining that you previously reported the entire gain from the condemnation. |  |
| [Publication 547, at 23](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p547.pdf) | Publication 547, Changing your mind (casualty gains). A taxpayer who reported the entire casualty gain and later buys replacement property files Form 1040-X and attaches an explanation that the entire gain was previously reported but only the part not reinvested is now being reported. | To postpone reporting your gain, file an amended return for 2024 using Form 1040-X. You should attach an explanation showing that you previously reported the entire gain from the casualty but you now want to report only the part of the gain ($1,000) equal to the part of the reimbursement not spent for replacement property. |  |
| [Publication 550, at 22](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p550.pdf) | Publication 550, Choosing to include market discount in income currently. A taxpayer electing under section 1278(b) attaches a statement to the timely filed return stating that market discount has been included in income and describing the method used to figure the accrued market discount. | Make the choice by attaching to your timely filed return a statement in which you: • State that you have included market discount in your gross income for the year under section 1278(b) of the Internal Revenue Code, and • Describe the method you used to figure the accrued market discount for the year. |  |
| [Publication 550, at 85](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p550.pdf) | Publication 550, How to report bad debts (Form 8949, Part I, box C). For each nonbusiness bad debt deducted as a short-term capital loss, attach a statement describing the debt (amount and due date), the debtor and any relationship to the debtor, efforts made to collect, and why the debt was deemed worthless. | For each bad debt, attach a statement to your return that contains: • A description of the debt, including the amount, and the date it became due; • The name of the debtor, and any business or family relationship between you and the debtor; … • The efforts you made to collect the debt; and • Why you decided the debt was worthless. |  |
| [Publication 557, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Withholding information from public inspection. An organization asking that trade secret or similar information in its exemption application be withheld from public inspection must identify the material and explain why it is the type of information that can be withheld. | Any information submitted in the application or in support of it that relates to any trade secret, patent, process, style of work, or apparatus, upon request, can be withheld from public inspection if the IRS determines that the disclosure of such information would adversely affect the organization. Your request must: 1. Identify the material to be withheld (the document, page, paragraph, and line) by clearly marking it “Not Subject to Public Inspection.” 2. Explain why the information is of the type that can be withheld from public inspection. |  |
| [Publication 557, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Appeal Procedures. An organization protesting a proposed adverse exemption determination must submit, within 30 days, a statement of its views fully explaining its reasoning and stating whether it wants Independent Office of Appeals consideration. | Your organization must submit a statement of its views fully explaining its reasoning. … The statement must be submitted within 30 days from the date of the proposed adverse determination letter and must state whether your organization wishes Independent Office of Appeals consideration. |  |
| [Publication 559, at 23](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p559.pdf) | Publication 559, Amended Return (Form 1041). A personal representative filing an amended Form 1041 explains on an attached sheet the reason for the changes and identifies the lines and amounts changed. | On an attached sheet, explain the reason for the changes and identify the lines and amounts changed. |  |
| [Publication 560, at 34](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p560.pdf) | Publication 560 (and Publication 575), recapture tax on substantially equal periodic payments. A taxpayer who owes the recapture tax and interest because payments under a qualifying distribution method were modified reports it on Form 5329, line 4, and attaches an explanation to the form. | Report the recapture tax and interest on line 4 of Form 5329. Attach an explanation to the form. | <sub>Publication 575, at 30</sub> |
| [Publication 575, at 26](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p575.pdf) | Publication 575, 5% rate on certain early distributions from deferred annuity contracts. A taxpayer who applies the 5% rate (rather than 10%) on Form 5329, line 4, for distributions under a written election in effect as of March 1, 1986, attaches an explanation to the return. | A 5% rate applies to distributions under a written election providing a specific schedule for the distribution of your interest in the contract if, as of March 1, 1986, you had begun receiving payments under the election. On line 4 of Form 5329, multiply the line 3 amount by 5% instead of 10%. Attach an explanation to your return. |  |
| [Publication 784, at 2](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p784.pdf) | Publication 784 (Certificate of Subordination of Federal Tax Lien), instructions for Form 14134, section 6325(d)(2) applications. An applicant under section 6325(d)(2) attaches a signed and dated statement describing how subordination will increase the amount the United States may ultimately realize and how it will facilitate collection. | For applications under section 6325(d)(2), complete and attach a signed and dated statement describing how the amount the United States may ultimately realize through this subordination will increase and how collection will be facilitated by the subordination. |  |
| [Publication 908, at 8](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p908.pdf) | Publication 908, Chapter 11 individual debtors (Notice 2006-83). A chapter 11 individual debtor attaches to Form 1040 a statement that the return is filed subject to a chapter 11 case, showing the allocation of income and withholding between the debtor and the estate and describing the method used to make that allocation. | IRS Notice 2006-83 requires the debtor to attach a statement to their individual income tax return (Form 1040 or 1040-SR) stating that the return is filed subject to a chapter 11 bankruptcy case. The statement must also: • Show the allocations of income and income tax withheld; • Describe the method used to allocate income and income tax withheld; and • List the filing date of the bankruptcy case, the bank- ruptcy court in which the case is pending, the bankruptcy court case number, and the bankruptcy estate's EIN. | <sub>Publication 525, at 3</sub> |
| [Publication 908, at 13](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p908.pdf) | Publication 908, Corporate bankruptcy. A trustee asking the IRS Insolvency Office for relief from filing income tax returns for a corporation that has ceased operations and has no assets or income must include a statement of facts showing why the debtor needs relief from the filing requirements. | The request to the IRS must include the name, address, and EIN of the corporation and a statement of the facts (with any supporting documents) showing why the debtor needs relief from the filing requirements. |  |
| [Publication 908, at 22](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p908.pdf) | Publication 908, Debt cancellation example (Form 982). A debtor who elects to reduce the basis of depreciable property for excluded canceled debt files Form 982 and attaches a statement describing the debt cancellation transaction and identifying the property whose basis is reduced. | Charlie must file Form 982 with the individual return (Form 1040 or 1040-SR) for the tax year of the debt discharge to reduce the depreciable basis of the property by $10,000. In addition, Charlie must attach a statement describing the debt cancellation transaction and identifying the property to which the basis reduction applies. |  |
| [Publication 926, at 12](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p926.pdf) | Publication 926, Correcting a stand-alone Schedule H. A household employer who files a corrected stand-alone Schedule H marks it “CORRECTED” and attaches a statement explaining the reason for the correction and the date the error was discovered. | In the top margin of your corrected Schedule H, write (in bold letters) “CORRECTED” followed by the date you discovered the error. In addition, explain the reason for your correction and the date the error was discovered in a statement attached to the corrected Schedule H. |  |
| [Publication 936, at 10](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p936.pdf) | Publication 936, How To Report, Schedule A (Form 1040), line 8b. If you paid more deductible mortgage interest than Form 1098 shows, report the omitted portion on line 8b, attach a statement to the paper return explaining the difference, and print “See attached” next to line 8b. | If you paid more deductible interest to the financial institution than the amount shown on Form 1098, show the portion of the deductible interest that was omitted from Form 1098 on line 8b. Attach a statement to your paper return explaining the difference and print “See attached” next to line 8b. |  |
| [Publication 946, at 10](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p946.pdf) | Publication 946, income forecast method, participations and residuals. To elect how participations and residuals will be treated, attach a statement to the timely filed return for the year the property is placed in service describing the property, the date placed in service, and the elected treatment. | To elect a method for the treatment of participations and residuals, attach a statement to the timely filed (including extensions) return for the tax year the income forecast property is placed in service providing a description of the property to which the participations and residuals relate, the date the property was placed in service, and how you elect to treat the participations and residuals for that property. |  |
| [Publication 1586, at 8](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p1586.pdf) | Publication 1586, response to Notice 972CG. A filer seeking waiver of a proposed information-return penalty must submit a signed written statement identifying the waiver provision and setting forth all facts supporting reasonable cause and showing it acted responsibly. | In seeking a waiver of the proposed penalty, the filer must submit a written statement that: 1. States the specific provision under which the waiver is being requested (for example: event beyond filer’s control), see Treas. Reg. 301.6724-1(b) and (c), 2. Sets forth all the facts alleged as the basis for reasonable cause and that the filer acted in a responsible manner (for example: specifies that the applicable solicitation (initial, first annual, second annual, etc.) for each missing/incorrect TIN took place in the time and manner required by regulations. See part VI for more information on TIN solicitation rules, as well as Treas. Reg. 301.6724-1(e) & (f)), 3. Contains the signature of person required to file the return, and 4. Contains the declaration that it is made under penalties of perjury. | <sub>Publication 1586, at 7</sub> |
| [Publication 1854, at 2](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p1854.pdf) | Publication 1854 (Form 433-A), Item 48 (Other Expenses). A taxpayer claiming other monthly expenses such as student loans or employment-required tuition attaches a list and explanation. | Item 48 – Other Expenses In certain circumstances, IRS may allow minimum payments on other expenses, such as student loans, unsecured debts, tuition or contributions if required for employment, etc. Attach a list and explanation. |  |
| [Publication 1915, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p1915.pdf) | Publication 1915 (ITIN), name changes. An ITIN holder whose legal name has changed requests the change by letter, explaining the circumstances leading to the change and enclosing supporting documentation. | Include an explanation of the circumstances leading to the change (marriage, divorce, etc.) and submit documentation to support your request. |  |
| [Publication 3498, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p3498.pdf) | Publication 3498, What if You Believe Your Bill Is Wrong. A taxpayer who writes to the IRS about a bill tells the IRS why the bill is believed to be wrong, enclosing supporting documents. | If you write to us, tell us why you believe your bill is wrong. With your letter, include copies of all the documents you gathered to explain your case. |  |
| [Publication 3498-A, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p3498a.pdf) | Publication 3498-A, question 4. A taxpayer under correspondence audit who wants a face-to-face interview must make the request in writing and explain why it is necessary. | You can call to discuss a face-to-face, and if your audit warrants one, your request must be in writing and explain why it is necessary. |  |
| [Publication 3833, at 9](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p3833.pdf) | Publication 3833 (Disaster Relief), requests for expedited processing of exemption applications. The request describes pending grants and how losing them would affect operations, any business emergency and how expediting would avoid it, and other consequences of denial. | a description of any pending grants, including information about the grantor and the amount or property to be received, an explanation of how the loss of the grant(s) might impact the organization’s ability to operate and provide relief, a description of any significant business emergency (such as an impending deadline imposed by a court or government agency) demonstrating that the business emergency will significantly impact the applicant’s ability to operate and explaining how expediting the application will enable the applicant to avoid the emergency, a statement explaining any other anticipated consequences should the expedited processing be denied, and , the date an exemption letter is required, if applicable. |  |
| [Publication 4078, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p4078.pdf) | Publication 4078 (Rev. Proc. 2002-48 relief for late Form 8038). An issuer of qualified private activity bonds requesting an extension for a late Form 8038 attaches a letter briefly explaining when the return was due, why it was not timely filed, and whether the bond issue is under examination. | These procedures generally require that the issuer: (1) attach a letter to the Form 8038 briefly explaining when the return was required to be filed, why the return was not timely submitted, and whether the bond issue is under examination; (2) enter on top of the letter “Request for Relief under section 3 of Rev. Proc. 2002-48”; and (3) file the letter and return at the Internal Revenue Service Center, Ogden UT 84201. | <sub>Publication 4079, at 9; Publication 5005, at 5; Form 8038-GC, at 2; Instructions for Form 8038-G, at 1</sub> |
| [Publication 4221-PF, at 20](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p4221pf.pdf) | Publication 4221-PF, Making Grants to Organizations or Individuals. A private foundation that plans a grant program not described in its exemption application should inform the IRS about the program. | Generally, if a private foundation intends to make grants to organizations or individuals and it did not describe the program in its exemption application, it should inform the IRS about the program. |  |
| [Publication 4227, at 2](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p4227.pdf) | Publication 4227 (Overview of the Appeals Process), taxpayer responsibilities. A taxpayer in Appeals must submit a statement listing each IRS position disputed and how the taxpayer understands the facts and law on each issue, and say how the case should be resolved. | It is your responsibility to: • Listen to our explanation of your appeal rights and the Appeals process • Submit a statement that includes a list of all IRS positions with which you disagree and how you understand the facts and law for each issue • Tell us how you think your case should be resolved |  |
| [Publication 5082, at 2](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p5082.pdf) | Publication 5082 (Chapter 13 Bankruptcy and Delinquent Tax Returns). A Chapter 13 debtor who receives an IRS letter requesting delinquent returns provides either signed copies of the returns or a written statement explaining why the debtor is not required to file. | If you receive a letter from IRS requesting to file one or more tax returns, please provide signed copies of the tax returns or a written statement explaining why you are not required to file. |  |
| [Publication 5149, at 1](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p5149.pdf) | Publication 5149, administrative return of levied property claim under IRC 6343(d). The taxpayer’s written claim states the grounds for return of the property and, if any required information is unavailable, includes a statement explaining why. | If any of that information is unavailable, your claim should include a statement explaining why. |  |
| [Publication 5181, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p5181.pdf) | Publication 5181 (Tax Return Reviews by Mail). A taxpayer who receives Notice CP2000 or Letter 2030 and disagrees with the proposed changes does not sign the agreement form and instead sends a written explanation of why they disagree, addressing each issue. | If you receive Notice CP2000 (or Letter 2030) and you don’t agree with the proposed changes: 1. Don’t sign the agreement form in the notice. 2. Explain why you disagree. Address each issue. Send your explanation and documentation in the envelope provided. |  |
| [Publication 5181, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p5181.pdf) | Publication 5181 (Tax Return Reviews by Mail). A taxpayer who receives Notice CP2501 or Letter 2531 and disagrees selects the disagree option on the Response form and includes supporting documentation and an explanation of why they disagree, addressing each item. | If you receive Notice CP2501 (or Letter 2531) and: … You disagree, complete the Response form. Select the “I don’t agree with some or all of the changes” option. Include supporting documentation and an explanation of why you disagree. Please address each item in the notice. |  |
| [Publication 5181, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p5181.pdf) | Publication 5181, Submitting documents. When responding to a CP2000-type notice, a taxpayer who cannot provide documents verifying an amount explains how the amount was determined. | If you’re unable to provide documents to verify an amount, explain how you determined the amount. |  |
| [Publication 5406, at 4](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p5406.pdf) | Publication 5406 (Overview of Form 1023 E-Filing), PDF upload. An organization that wants expedited handling of its Form 1023 application includes in its uploaded PDF a written explanation of why the application should be expedited. | Finally, if you want to request expedited handling of your application, include a written explanation as to why your application should be expedited in your PDF. | <sub>Publication 5470, at 5; Publication 5632, at 5</sub> |
| [Publication 6069, at 11](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p6069.pdf) | Publication 6069 (48(e) Low-Income Communities Bonus Credit Program FAQs), Q46. An applicant for a capacity limitation allocation that is not a party named in the executed customer contract or interconnection agreement provides a statement explaining why it is not named and the relationship between the named entity and the applicant. | However, if the applicant is not a party named in the contract, the applicant must provide with the applicable contract a statement explaining why the applicant is not named in the contract and the relationship between the appropriate entity named in the contract and the applicant—the latter of which must be the owner of the facility to be eligible to apply for an allocation of Capacity Limitation. |  |
| [Publication 7951, at 2](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p7951.pdf) | Publication 7951, Q&A 9. A payer that receives Letter 6112 proposing backup withholding tax for missing payee TINs and that did not file Form 945 under its own EIN responds either with a signed Form 945 or with a signed statement explaining why it believes it is not required to file. | Respond to Letter 6112 by providing a signed Form 945 for the amount of backup withholding shown on the enclosed list of payees, or send us a signed statement explaining why you believe you’re not required to file. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.79-2(b)(4)(ii)(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.79-2#p-1.79-2(b)(4)(ii)(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.79-2 (exception to inclusion of group-term life insurance cost for disabled former employees). For the first year an individual claims the section 79(b)(1) disability exception, the individual submits with the return a doctor’s statement about the impairment and the individual’s own statement on the impairment’s effect on substantial gainful activity and when it occurred. | For the first taxable year for which the individual seeks to apply the exception set forth in section 79(b)(1) by reason of his being disabled within the meaning of subdivision (i) of this subparagraph, there must be submitted with his income tax return a doctor's statement as to his impairment. There must also be submitted with the return a statement by the individual with respect to the effect of the impairment upon his substantial gainful activity, and the date such impairment occurred. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.108-2(c)(4)(iv)(E)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.108-2#p-1.108-2(c)(4)(iv)(E)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.108-2(c)(4)(iv). When disclosure is required because a holder acquired the debtor’s indebtedness within the specified period before becoming related to the debtor, the debtor’s disclosure statement must include, among other items, a statement describing the facts and circumstances supporting the debtor’s position that the holder did not acquire the indebtedness in anticipation of becoming related to the debtor. | A statement under this paragraph (c)(4) must include the following— (A) A caption identifying the statement as disclosure under § 1.108-2(c); (B) An identification of the indebtedness with respect to which disclosure is made; (C) The amount of such indebtedness and the amount of income from discharge of indebtedness is section 108(e)(4) were to apply; (D) Whether paragraph (c)(4)(ii) or (iii) of this section applies to the transaction; and (E) A statement describing the facts and circumstances supporting the debtor's position that the holder did not acquire the indebtedness in anticipation of becoming related to the debtor. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.162-24(e)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.162-24#p-1.162-24(e)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.162-24(e)(3) (revocation of a state legislator’s section 162(h) election for travel expenses). A legislator seeking IRS consent to revoke the election files a signed application with the processing center where the election was filed, including a statement explaining why the taxpayer seeks to revoke it. | An application for consent to revoke an election must be signed by the taxpayer and filed with the submission processing center with which the election was filed, and must include— (i) The taxpayer's name, address, and taxpayer identification number; (ii) A statement that the taxpayer is revoking an election under section 162(h) for a specified year; and (iii) A statement explaining why the taxpayer seeks to revoke the election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.172-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.172-1#p-1.172-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.172-1(c) (net operating loss deduction). A taxpayer claiming an NOL deduction attaches to the return a concise statement of the amount claimed and all material and pertinent facts, including a detailed schedule computing the deduction. | Every taxpayer claiming a net operating loss deduction for any taxable year shall file with his return for such year a concise statement setting forth the amount of the net operating loss deduction claimed and all material and pertinent facts relative thereto, including a detailed schedule showing the computation of the net operating loss deduction. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.174-4(b)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.174-4#p-1.174-4(b)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.174-4(b)(2) (change in the treatment of research or experimental expenditures deferred under section 174(b)). A taxpayer applying for permission to change the amortization period or method must state the new period or method proposed, the reasons for the change, and information identifying the affected projects. | In addition, the application shall set forth the length of the new period or periods proposed, or the new method of treatment proposed, the reasons for the proposed change, and such information as will identify the project or projects to which the expenditures affected by the change relate. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.179-5(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.179-5#p-1.179-5(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.179-5(b) (revocation of a section 179 expensing election requiring IRS consent). A taxpayer requesting consent to revoke the election files a signed request with a statement showing the year and property involved and setting forth in detail the reasons for the request. | It must be accompanied by a statement showing the year and property involved, and must set forth in detail the reasons for the request. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.182-6(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.182-6#p-1.182-6(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.182-6(b) (revocation of an election to deduct land clearing expenditures). A taxpayer seeking consent to revoke the section 182 election sends the IRS a letter giving its identifying information, the year involved, and the reasons for the revocation. | Requests for consent to revoke an election under section 182 shall be made by means of a letter to the district director for the district in which the taxpayer is required to file his return, setting forth the taxpayer's name, address and identification number, the year for which it is desired to revoke the election, and the reasons therefor. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.194-4(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.194-4#p-1.194-4(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.194-4(c) (revocation of a section 194 election to amortize reforestation expenditures). A taxpayer seeking consent to revoke the election files a signed written application with the IRS stating the taxable years the election was in effect and the reason for revoking it. | The application shall set forth the name and address of the taxpayer, state the taxable years for which the election was in effect, and state the reason for revoking the election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-2(j)(2)(ii)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-2#p-1.367(a)-2(j)(2)(ii)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-2(j)(2)(ii)(A) (outbound transfers of property for use in an active trade or business). A taxpayer seeking to show that its failure to comply was not willful files an amended return with the missing information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure, and may include a request for section 6038B penalty relief in the same submission. | A taxpayer's statement that the failure to comply was not willful will be considered only if, promptly after the taxpayer becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-3(f)(2)(ii)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-3#p-1.367(a)-3(f)(2)(ii)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-3(f)(2)(ii)(A) (outbound transfers of stock or securities to foreign corporations). A taxpayer seeking to show that its failure to file was not willful files an amended return with the missing information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to file. | A taxpayer's statement that the failure to file was not willful will be considered only if, promptly after the taxpayer becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to file. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-7(e)(2)(ii)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-7#p-1.367(a)-7(e)(2)(ii)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-7(e)(2)(ii)(A) (outbound transfers of property in section 361 exchanges). A control group member or U.S. transferor seeking to show that its failure to comply was not willful files an amended return with the missing information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure, and may include a section 6038B penalty relief request in the same submission. | A control group member or U.S. transferor's statement that the failure to comply was not willful will be considered only if, promptly after the control group member or U.S. transferor, as applicable, becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(c)(2)(viii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-8#p-1.367(a)-8(c)(2)(viii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(c)(2)(viii). A U.S. transferor entering into a gain recognition agreement for a transfer of stock or securities to a foreign corporation includes in the agreement a statement describing any disposition of the transferred corporation’s assets during the taxable year outside the ordinary course of business. | (viii) A statement describing any disposition of assets of the transferred corporation during such taxable year other than in the ordinary course of business. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(c)(3)(vi)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-8#p-1.367(a)-8(c)(3)(vi)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(c)(3)(vi). In a gain recognition agreement for a transfer of stock of a domestic corporation to a foreign corporation, the U.S. transferor includes a statement describing how section 7874 (inversions) applies to the transaction and indicating that the requirements of § 1.367(a)-3(c)(1) are satisfied. | (vi) If the transferred corporation is domestic, a statement describing the application of section 7874 to the transaction, and indicating that the requirements of § 1.367(a)-3(c)(1) are satisfied. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(d)(2)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-8#p-1.367(a)-8(d)(2)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(d)(2)(ii). Where an initial transfer and one or more later dispositions or other events affecting a gain recognition agreement occur in the same year, the U.S. transferor files a single gain recognition agreement that describes the initial transfer and each disposition or other event affecting the agreement. | The gain recognition agreement must describe the initial transfer and/or each disposition or other event that affects the gain recognition agreement (even if a triggering event exception applies). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(g)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-8#p-1.367(a)-8(g)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(g). In its annual certification under a gain recognition agreement, the U.S. transferor gives a complete description of any event that terminated or reduced the gain subject to the agreement and a statement describing any disposition of the transferred corporation’s assets outside the ordinary course of business. | (2) A complete description of any event occurring during such taxable year that has terminated or reduced the amount of gain subject to the gain recognition agreement (for example, an event described in paragraph (o) of this section), including a calculation of any reduction to the amount of gain subject to the gain recognition agreement. (3) A statement describing any disposition of assets of the transferred corporation during the taxable year not in the ordinary course of business. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(p)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(a)-8#p-1.367(a)-8(p)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(a)-8(p)(2)(i) (gain recognition agreements). A U.S. transferor seeking to show that a failure to file or comply with GRA requirements was not willful files an amended return with the missing information, a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure, and a Form 8838 extending the assessment period. | A U.S. transferor's statement that a failure to file or failure to comply was not willful will be considered only if, promptly after the U.S. transferor becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to file or failure to comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(b)-1(c)(4)(viii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(b)-1#p-1.367(b)-1(c)(4)(viii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(b)-1(c)(4)(viii)–(ix). A person filing a section 367(b) notice for a triangular acquisition describes the acquisition of the parent’s stock and any adjustments made under § 1.367(b)-10 or explains why none were made, and, where § 1.367(b)-3(g) applies, describes how any excess asset basis arose and how it was computed. | The section 367(b) notice must also include a complete description of the acquisition of the stock or securities of P in exchange for property, including a description of the property provided in exchange for the stock or securities and any related transactions involving the acquisition of the stock or securities. The section 367(b) notice must describe any adjustments made pursuant to § 1.367(b)-10 or, if no adjustments are made, explain why no such adjustments were made; and (ix) In the case of an exchange to which § 1.367(b)-3(g) applies, a statement describing how any excess asset basis (as defined in § 1.367(b)-3(g)(2)(i)) arose, the amount of excess asset basis, and a description of the computation of the amount of excess asset basis. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.367(e)-2(f)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.367(e)-2#p-1.367(e)-2(f)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.367(e)-2(f)(2)(i) (section 367(e)(2) liquidating distributions to foreign corporations). A taxpayer seeking to show that a failure to file or comply was not willful files an amended return with the missing information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure. | A taxpayer's statement that a failure to file or failure to comply was not willful will be considered only if, promptly after the taxpayer becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.404(a)-2(b)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.404(a)-2#p-1.404(a)-2(b)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.404(a)-2(b)(1) (employer information on pension, annuity, or profit-sharing plans in later years). After all initial plan information has been filed, if the plan, instruments, methods, factors, or assumptions underlying certain required data change, the employer files a detailed statement explaining the change and its effect for the year the change takes effect; otherwise it files a statement that there was no change. | If there is any change in the plan, instruments, methods, factors, or assumptions upon which the data and information specified in paragraph (a) (1), (2), or (7) of this section are based, a detailed statement explaining the change and its effect is to be filed only for the taxable year in which the change is put into effect. However, if there is no such change, unless otherwise requested by the district director, merely a statement that there is no such change is to be filed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.404(a)-2(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.404(a)-2#p-1.404(a)-2(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.404(a)-2(d). An employer claiming a deduction for contributions to a pension, annuity, or profit-sharing plan that cannot file the required supporting information with its return may file it within 12 months after year end if it attaches to the return a statement that the information cannot reasonably be filed with the return, setting forth the reasons. | All the information required by this section should be filed with the tax return for the taxable year in which the deduction is claimed, except that, unless sooner requested by the district director, such information, other than that specified in paragraph (a)(4)(i) and (9) of this section, may be filed within 12 months after the close of the taxable year provided there is filed with the tax return a statement that the information cannot reasonably be filed therewith, setting forth the reasons therefor. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.448-1(h)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.448-1#p-1.448-1(h)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.448-1(h)(2). A taxpayer required by section 448 to change from the cash method to an overall accrual method files Form 3115 and attaches a statement giving the period over which the section 481(a) adjustment will be taken into account and the basis for that conclusion. | In addition, the taxpayer must set forth on a statement accompanying the Form 3115 the period over which the section 481(a) adjustment will be taken into account and the basis for such conclusion. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.448-3(d)(6)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.448-3#p-1.448-3(d)(6)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.448-3(d)(6). A taxpayer using a nonaccrual-experience method that wants approval to exclude a prior year’s bad debt experience requests a letter ruling that includes a statement of the reasons the experience should be excluded. | A request for approval to exclude the experience of a prior taxable year must be made in accordance with the applicable procedures for requesting a letter ruling and must include a statement of the reasons the experience should be excluded. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.451-7(g)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.451-7#p-1.451-7(g)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.451-7(g). A taxpayer electing under section 451(e) to defer income from livestock sold early because of drought attaches a statement to its return that, among other things, explains the relationship of the drought area to the early sale or exchange of the livestock. | It must be made by attaching a statement to the return or an amended return for such taxable year. The statement shall include the name and address of the taxpayer and shall set forth the following information for each classification of livestock for which the election is made: … (3) A statement explaining the relationship of the drought area to the taxpayer's early sale or exchange of the livestock; |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.469-9(g)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.469-9#p-1.469-9(g)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.469-9(g)(3). A real estate professional revoking the election to treat all rental real estate interests as a single activity (allowed only after a material change in facts and circumstances) files a statement with the original return for the revocation year declaring the revocation and explaining the nature of the material change. | To revoke the election, the taxpayer must file a statement with the taxpayer's original income tax return for the year of revocation. This statement must contain a declaration that the taxpayer is revoking the election under section 469(c)(7)(A) and an explanation of the nature of the material change. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.472-8(e)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.472-8#p-1.472-8(e)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.472-8(e)(1). A dollar-value LIFO taxpayer that uses an index or link-chain method attaches to its return for the first year the method is used a statement describing the link-chain method or the method used to compute the index, in enough detail to determine whether the method meets the regulatory standards, and files a copy with the Commissioner. | A taxpayer using either an index or link-chain method shall attach to his income tax return for the first taxable year beginning after December 31, 1960, for which the index or link-chain method is used, a statement describing the particular link-chain method or the method used in computing the index. The statement shall be in sufficient detail to facilitate the determination as to whether the method used meets the standards set forth in this subparagraph. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.475(a)-4(k)(2)(i)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.475(a)-4#p-1.475(a)-4(k)(2)(i)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.475(a)-4(k)(2)(i)(A) (mark-to-market valuation safe harbor). A taxpayer using its applicable financial statement values under section 475 must provide reconciliation schedules between the financial statement and the tax return, including a detailed explanation of any adjustments caused by the imperfect overlap between the positions marked to market for tax purposes and those valued under an eligible method on the financial statement. | The required reconciliation schedules must also include a detailed explanation of any adjustments necessitated by the imperfect overlap between the eligible positions that the taxpayer marks to market under section 475 and the eligible positions for which the applicable financial statement uses an eligible method. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.481-4(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.481-4#p-1.481-4(a)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.481-4(a). A taxpayer changing its method of accounting that requests approval of an alternative method of allocating the section 481 adjustments must set out in detail the facts and circumstances on which it bases the request. | Requests for approval of an alternative method of allocation shall set forth in detail the facts and circumstances upon which the taxpayer bases its request. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.534-2(d)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.534-2#p-1.534-2(d)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.534-2(d)(2). A taxpayer that cannot, for good cause, submit its section 534(c) statement within 60 days after the notification may obtain up to 30 more days by filing, before the 60-day period ends, a request setting out the reasons for the extension. | If the taxpayer is unable, for good cause, to submit the statement within such 60-day period, an additional period not exceeding 30 days may be granted upon receipt in the Internal Revenue office concerned (before the expiration of the 60-day period provided herein) of a request from the taxpayer, setting forth the reasons for such request. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.545-2(h)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.545-2#p-1.545-2(h)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.545-2(h)(2). A personal holding company that claims expenses and depreciation for property it owns or operates in excess of the rent or other compensation received for the property must attach to its return a statement of its claim for the additional deductions, with a complete statement of the pertinent facts and circumstances and the arguments it relies on (including, for example, the reasons no rent was received and the purpose for which the property was used). | If, in computing undistributed personal holding company income, a personal holding company claims deductions for expenses and depreciation allocable to the operation and maintenance of property owned or operated by the company, in an aggregate amount in excess of the rent or other compensation received for the use of, or the right to use, the property, it shall attach to its income tax return a statement setting forth its claim for allowance of the additional deductions, together with a complete statement of the facts and circumstances pertinent to its claim and the arguments on which it relies. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.561-2(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.561-2#p-1.561-2(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.561-2(c). A corporation claiming a dividends paid deduction files with its return a copy of the dividend resolution and a concise statement of the pertinent facts about the payment, specifying the medium of payment and, for property distributions, the fair market value and adjusted basis of the property and how they were determined. | Such corporation shall file with its return (i) a copy of the dividend resolution; and (ii) a concise statement of the pertinent facts relating to the payment of the dividend, clearly specifying ( a ) the medium of payment and ( b ) if not paid in money, the fair market value and adjusted basis (or face value, if paid in its own obligations) on the date of distribution of the property distributed and the manner in which such fair market value and adjusted basis were determined. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.564-1(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.564-1#p-1.564-1(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.564-1(d). A personal holding company claiming a dividend carryover files with its return a concise statement of the amount claimed and all material and pertinent facts, including a detailed schedule showing how the carryover was computed. | Every corporation claiming a dividend carryover for any taxable year shall file with its return for such year a concise statement setting forth the amount of the dividend carryover claimed and all material and pertinent facts relative thereto, including a detailed schedule showing the computation of the dividend carryover claimed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.613-4(h)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.613-4#p-1.613-4(h)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.613-4(h). A mineral taxpayer applying to the Commissioner to treat transportation of more than 50 miles as mining for percentage depletion includes a statement setting forth in detail the facts about the physical and other requirements that prevented building and operating the processing plant nearer to the point of extraction. | The application must include a statement setting forth in detail the facts concerning the physical and other requirements which prevented the construction and operation of the plant (in which mining processes, as defined in paragraph (f) of this section, are applied) at a place nearer to the point of extraction from the ground. These facts must be sufficient to apprise the Commissioner of the exact basis of the application. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-2(d)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-2#p-1.614-2(d)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-2(d)(2)(i). A taxpayer electing under section 614(b) to aggregate operating mineral interests within an operating unit attaches a statement to its return describing the aggregation, the interests to be treated separately, and the operating unit in sufficient detail to show the aggregated interests are properly within a single operating unit. | This statement shall indicate that the taxpayer is making an aggregation of separate operating mineral interests within an operating unit under section 614(b) and shall contain a description of the aggregation and describe the operating mineral interests within the operating unit which are to be treated as separate properties apart from the aggregation. … The statement shall also contain a description of the operating unit in sufficient detail to show that the aggregated operating mineral interests are properly within a single operating unit. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-2(d)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-2#p-1.614-2(d)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-2 (election to aggregate operating mineral interests under section 614(b)). A taxpayer bound by the election that seeks the Commissioner’s consent to change the treatment of an operating mineral interest applies in writing with a statement of the reasons for the change and the required property information. | The application must be accompanied by a statement indicating the reason or reasons for the change and furnishing the information required under subdivision (i) of subparagraph (2) of this paragraph, unless such information has been previously filed and is current. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-3(b)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-3#p-1.614-3(b)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-3 (election under section 614(c)(2) to treat an operating mineral interest developed by two or more mines as separate interests). A taxpayer seeking the Commissioner’s consent to make the election for an aggregated property applies in writing with a statement setting out in detail the reasons for the request. | The application must be accompanied by a statement setting forth in detail the reason or reasons for the request to exercise the election with respect to an aggregated property. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-3(f)(7)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-3#p-1.614-3(f)(7)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-3(f)(7). A taxpayer seeking the Commissioner’s consent to change the treatment of a mine operating mineral interest covered by a section 614(c) election (adding it to or removing it from an aggregation) applies in writing with a statement of the reasons for the change and the required property information. | Applications for consent shall be made in writing to the Commissioner of Internal Revenue, Washington, DC 20224. The application must be accompanied by a statement indicating the reason or reasons for the change and furnishing the information required in subparagraph (5)(i) of this paragraph, unless such information has been previously filed and is current. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(b)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-5#p-1.614-5(b)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(b)(2) (aggregation under paragraph (a)). A taxpayer applying for permission to aggregate nonoperating mineral interests in a tract of land must include a complete statement of the facts showing the undue hardship that would result if aggregation were not permitted, together with a description of the interests (a general description with marked maps is sufficient). | The application for permission to aggregate nonoperating mineral interests under paragraph (a) of this section shall include a complete statement of the facts upon which the taxpayer relies to show the undue hardship which would result if such an aggregation was not permitted. Such application shall also include a description of the nonoperating mineral interests owned by the taxpayer within the tract or tracts of land involved. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(b)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-5#p-1.614-5(b)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(b)(3). A taxpayer bound by an election to aggregate nonoperating mineral interests that seeks the Commissioner’s consent to change that treatment must file an application setting out in detail the reasons for the change. | The application for consent to make a change must set forth in detail the reason or reasons for such change. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(e)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-5#p-1.614-5(e)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(e)(4). A taxpayer applying under section 614(e) and paragraph (d) to aggregate separate nonoperating mineral interests must include a complete statement of the facts showing that avoidance of tax is not a principal purpose of forming the aggregation. | The application for permission to aggregate nonoperating mineral interests under section 614(e) and paragraph (d) of this section shall include a complete statement of the facts upon which the taxpayer relies to show that avoidance of tax is not a principal purpose of forming the aggregation. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(e)(5)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-5#p-1.614-5(e)(5)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-5(e)(5). A taxpayer bound by an election under section 614(e) to aggregate separate nonoperating mineral interests that seeks the Commissioner’s consent to change that treatment must file an application setting out in detail the reasons for the change. | The application for consent to make a change must set forth in detail the reason or reasons for such change. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.614-8(a)(3)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.614-8#p-1.614-8(a)(3)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.614-8(a)(3)(ii). A taxpayer electing to treat oil and gas operating mineral interests within the same tract or parcel as separate properties or in combination attaches a statement identifying the interests and the tract and setting forth the facts on which treatment of the tract as a single and entire tract or parcel is based. | The statement shall also identify by name, code number, or other means the tract or parcel and shall set forth the facts upon which its treatment as a single and entire tract or parcel is based. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.632-1(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.632-1#p-1.632-1(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.632-1(b). A taxpayer limiting the tax on the sale of oil or gas property it discovered or developed under section 632 must submit with the return a statement fully explaining how expenses, losses, and deductions were allocated or apportioned between the sale and other income. | The taxpayer shall submit with his return a statement fully explaining the manner in which such expenses, losses, and deductions are allocated or apportioned. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.636-4(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.636-4#p-1.636-4(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.636-4(d). A taxpayer seeking the Commissioner’s consent to revoke its section 503(c)(2) election (production payments) must apply in writing setting out the reasons for revocation and a recomputation of tax for each affected prior year. | Such application must set forth the reasons therefor and a recomputation of the tax reflecting such revocation for each prior taxable year affected by the revocation, whether or not the period of limitations for credit or refund or assessment and collection has expired with respect to such taxable year. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.642(c)-6(c)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.642(c)-6#p-1.642(c)-6(c)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.642(c)-6(c)(4). A pooled income fund must attach to its income tax return for each taxable year a full statement supporting the method it used to calculate its yearly rate of return. | (4) A pooled income fund's method of calculating its yearly rate of return must be supported by a full statement attached to the income tax return of the pooled income fund for each taxable year. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.721(c)-6(f)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.721(c)-6#p-1.721(c)-6(f)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.721(c)-6(f)(2)(i). A U.S. transferor seeking to show that a failure to comply with the gain deferral method’s reporting requirements was not willful must promptly file an amended return with the omitted information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to comply. | A U.S. transferor's statement that a failure to comply was not willful will be considered only if, promptly after the U.S. transferor becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section as well as a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.754-1(c)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.754-1#p-1.754-1(c)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.754-1(c)(1). A partnership seeking to revoke its section 754 election files an application, signed by a partner within 30 days after the close of the year, setting forth the grounds on which revocation is desired. | A partnership which wishes to revoke such an election shall file with the district director for the internal revenue district in which the partnership return is required to be filed an application setting forth the grounds on which the revocation is desired. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.761-2(d)(4)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.761-2#p-1.761-2(d)(4)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.761-2(d)(4)(ii). Co-producers of natural gas under a joint operating agreement who request permission to use the annual method must submit a written request naming all co-producers and explaining how they will report gas sale income, balancing payments, production expenses, depletion, and production credits. | In addition, the request must contain an explanation of how the co-producers will report income from gas sales, the making or receiving of a balancing payment, production expenses, depletion deductions, and production credits. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.811-2(c)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.811-2#p-1.811-2(c)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.811-2(c)(4). A life insurance company claiming a deduction for dividends to policyholders files with its return a concise statement of the facts relating to its dividend policy, dividends actually paid, and amounts held or set aside as reserves for policyholder dividends. | The company shall file with its return a concise statement of the pertinent facts relating to its dividend policy for the year, the amount of dividends actually paid during the taxable year, and the amounts held or set aside as reserves for dividends to policyholders during the taxable year. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.812-2(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.812-2#p-1.812-2(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.812-2(c). A life insurance company claiming an operations loss deduction files with its return a concise statement of the deduction claimed and all material facts, including a detailed schedule of its computation. | Every life insurance company claiming an operations loss deduction for any taxable year shall file with its return for such year a concise statement setting forth the amount of the operations loss deduction claimed and all material and pertinent facts relative thereto, including a detailed schedule showing the computation of the operations loss deduction. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.861-8(e)(6)(ii)(C)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.861-8#p-1.861-8(e)(6)(ii)(C)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.861-8(e)(6)(ii)(C)(2). A taxpayer that allocates and apportions its deduction for state income taxes using a method other than those illustrated in the regulation’s examples must describe the alternative method on an attachment to its federal income tax return (and on examination show that it more accurately reflects the relationship between the state tax and the income taxed). | A taxpayer that uses a method of allocation and apportionment other than one illustrated in Example 25 (as modified by Examples 26 and 27), or 29 with respect to a factual situation similar to those of the examples, must describe the alternative method on an attachment to its federal income tax return and establish to the satisfaction of the District Director, upon examination, that the result of the alternative method more accurately reflects the factual relationship between the state income tax and the income on which the tax is imposed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.861-15(g)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.861-15#p-1.861-15(g)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.861-15(g)(2). A taxpayer seeking the Commissioner’s consent to revoke its section 861(e) election (treating income from certain U.S.-built aircraft or vessels leased to U.S. persons as U.S.-source) must file a signed written request specifying the years affected and the grounds considered to justify the revocation. | The request must specify the grounds which are considered to justify the revocation. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.863-1(b)(6)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.863-1#p-1.863-1(b)(6)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.863-1(b)(6). A taxpayer that sources income from natural resources under paragraph (b)(2) attaches a statement to its return explaining the methodology used to determine fair market value and any additional production activities it performed. | (6) Tax return disclosure. A taxpayer that determines the source of its income under paragraph (b)(2) of this section shall attach a statement to its return explaining the methodology used to determine fair market value under paragraph (b)(4) of this section, and explaining any additional production activities (as defined in paragraph (b)(3)(ii) of this section) performed by the taxpayer. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.863-3A(b)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.863-3A#p-1.863-3A(b)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.863-3A (Example 1, independent factory price). Where a manufacturer computes U.S.-source taxable income by treating products as sold at an established independent factory price, the basis of that accounting must be fully explained in a statement attached to the return. | In all such cases the basis of the accounting shall be fully explained in a statement attached to the return for the taxable year. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.871-10(d)(2)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.871-10#p-1.871-10(d)(2)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.871-10, written request required. A nonresident alien or foreign corporation seeking the Commissioner’s consent to revoke, or newly make, an election to treat real property income as effectively connected files a signed written request specifying the taxable year and the grounds that justify the revocation or new election. | The request shall include the name and address of the taxpayer and shall be signed by the taxpayer or his duly authorized representative. It must specify the taxable year for which the revocation or new election is to be effective and shall be filed within 75 days after the close of the first taxable year for which it is desired to make the change. The request must specify the grounds which are considered to justify the revocation or new election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.884-5(b)(3)(v)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.884-5#p-1.884-5(b)(3)(v)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.884-5(b)(3)(v). A foreign corporation that failed to obtain qualified-resident documentation on time may perfect it late if, before an examination, it shows reasonable cause by a written statement giving the reasons for the failure and describing the documentation received after the deadline. | It may make such a showing by providing a written statement to the District Director having jurisdiction over the taxpayer's return or the Office of the Assistant Commissioner (International), as applicable, setting forth the reasons for the failure to obtain the documentation in a timely manner and describing the documentation that was received after the deadline had passed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.904-2(f)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.904-2#p-1.904-2(f)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.904-2(f). A taxpayer claiming a carryback or carryover of unused foreign tax attaches to Form 1116 or 1118 a statement setting forth the unused foreign tax and all material facts, including a detailed schedule of its computation. | Every taxpayer claiming the benefit of a carryback or carryover of the unused foreign tax to any taxable year for which he chooses to claim a credit under section 901 shall file with his return (or with his claim for refund, if appropriate) for that year as an attachment to his Form 1116 or 1118, as the case may be, a statement setting forth the unused foreign tax deemed paid or accrued under this section and all material and pertinent facts relative thereto, including a detailed schedule showing the computation of the unused foreign tax so carried back or over. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.951A-3(h)(1)(iv)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.951A-3#p-1.951A-3(h)(1)(iv)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.951A-3(h)(1)(iv)(A). To rebut the presumption that specified tangible property held by a CFC for less than 12 months was acquired temporarily to increase deemed tangible income return, attach a statement to Form 5471 for the year of the subsequent transfer explaining the facts and circumstances supporting the rebuttal. | In order to rebut the presumption, a statement must be attached to the Form 5471 filed by the taxpayer for the taxable year of the CFC in which the subsequent transfer occurs and include any information required by applicable administrative announcements, forms or instructions. The statement must explain the facts and circumstances supporting the rebuttal and be in accordance with any rules provided in forms and instructions. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.981-1(c)(2)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.981-1#p-1.981-1(c)(2)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.981-1 (termination of election for foreign law community income). Spouses seeking to terminate the election for later years file a signed written request specifying the years affected and the grounds justifying termination. | The request must include the name, address, and account number, if any, of each spouse and must be signed by the persons making the request. It must specify the taxable year or years for which the termination is to be effective and the grounds which justify the termination. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1092(b)-4T(f)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1092(b)-4T#p-1.1092(b)-4T(f)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1092(b)-4T(f)(2)(i). A taxpayer electing to establish a mixed straddle account on Form 6781 must attach to the form a statement designating with specificity the class of activities covered, in enough detail for the IRS to determine which positions belong in the account. | In addition, the taxpayer must attach a statement to Form 6781 designating with specificity the class of activities for which a mixed straddle account is established. The designation must describe the class of activities in sufficient detail so that the Commissioner may determine, on the basis of the designation, whether specific positions are includible in the mixed straddle account. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1248(f)-3(a)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1248(f)-3#p-1.1248(f)-3(a)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1248(f)-3(a)(2)(i). A reporting person seeking to show that a failure to timely comply with the § 1.1248(f)-2 reporting requirements was due to reasonable cause must promptly file an amended return with the omitted information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure. | A reporting person's statement that the failure to timely comply was due to reasonable cause and not willful neglect will be considered only if, promptly after the reporting person becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to timely comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1273-2(f)(9)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1273-2#p-1.1273-2(f)(9)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1273-2(f)(9)(i). A debt holder that disagrees with the issuer’s determination of whether property is traded on an established market (or its fair market value) must explicitly disclose that on its timely filed return for the year it acquired the debt instrument, describing the reasons for its different determination and how it determined fair market value. | A holder must describe in the disclosure the reasons for its different determination and, if applicable, how the holder determined the fair market value. A holder's disclosure must be filed on a timely filed Federal income tax return for the taxable year that includes the acquisition date of the debt instrument. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1295-3(c)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1295-3#p-1.1295-3(c)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1295-3(c)(2). A shareholder filing a PFIC Protective Statement must include a reasonable belief statement describing its basis for believing the foreign corporation was not a PFIC, discussing the application of the income and asset tests and the factors that affect them. | The Protective Statement must contain a reasonable belief statement, as described in paragraph (c)(1) of this section. The reasonable belief statement is a description of the shareholder's basis for its reasonable belief that the foreign corporation was not a PFIC for its taxable year that ended with or within the shareholder's first taxable year to which the Protective Statement applies. … The reasonable belief statement must discuss the application of the income and asset tests to the foreign corporation and the factors, including those stated in paragraph (d) of this section, that affect the results of those tests. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1295-3(f)(4)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1295-3#p-1.1295-3(f)(4)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1295-3(f)(4)(ii).
