A PFIC shareholder requesting the Commissioner’s consent to make a retroactive QEF (section 1295) election files a ruling request with a detailed affidavit describing the events that led to the failure to make a timely election and its discovery, the engagement and responsibilities of the qualified tax professional, and how far the shareholder relied on that professional. | The shareholder, or a person authorized to sign a Federal income tax return on behalf of the shareholder, must submit a detailed affidavit describing the events that led to the failure to make a section 1295 election by the election due date, and to the discovery thereof. The shareholder's affidavit must describe the engagement and responsibilities of the qualified tax professional as well as the extent to which the shareholder relied on the tax professional. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-10, section 4.02(2)(c), 2026-4 I.R.B. 394, at 398</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1295-3(f)(4)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1295-3#p-1.1295-3(f)(4)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1295-3(f)(4)(iii). With a request for consent to make a retroactive section 1295 election, the shareholder must also submit detailed affidavits from individuals with knowledge of the events (including the tax professional relied on), each describing those events, the individual’s engagement and responsibilities, and the advice given about the foreign corporation. | The shareholder must submit detailed affidavits from individuals having knowledge or information about the events that led to the failure to make a section 1295 election by the election due date, and to the discovery thereof. These individuals must include the qualified tax professional upon whose advice the shareholder relied, as well as any individual (including an employee of the shareholder) who made a substantial contribution to the return's preparation, and any accountant or attorney, knowledgeable in tax matters, who advised the shareholder with regard to its ownership of the stock of the foreign corporation. Each affidavit must describe the individual's engagement and responsibilities as well as the advice concerning the tax treatment of the foreign corporation that that individual provided to the shareholder. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-10, section 4.02(2)(d), 2026-4 I.R.B. 394, at 398</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1347-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1347-1#p-1.1347-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1347-1(c). A taxpayer computing the section 1347 limitation on tax for certain claims against the United States allocates expenses, losses, and deductions to the amount received and submits with the return a statement fully explaining how they were allocated or apportioned. | The taxpayer shall submit with his return a statement fully explaining the manner in which such expenses, losses, and deductions are allocated or apportioned. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1361-1(j)(11)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1361-1#p-1.1361-1(j)(11)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1361-1(j)(11). A current income beneficiary seeking the Commissioner’s consent to revoke a QSST election submits a letter ruling request that explains why the beneficiary seeks to revoke the election and indicates that the beneficiary understands the consequences. | The application must be signed by the current income beneficiary and must— (i) Contain the name, address, and taxpayer identification number of the current income beneficiary, the trust, and the corporation with respect to which the QSST election was made; (ii) Identify the election being revoked as an election made under section 1361(d)(2); and (iii) Explain why the current income beneficiary seeks to revoke the QSST election and indicate that the beneficiary understands the consequences of the revocation. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1362-4(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1362-4#p-1.1362-4(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1362-4(c). An S corporation seeking a determination that the termination or invalidity of its election was inadvertent submits a ruling request setting forth all relevant facts, including a detailed explanation of the terminating event, when and how it was discovered, and the corrective steps taken. | The request is made in the form of a ruling request and should set forth all relevant facts pertaining to the event or circumstance including, but not limited to, the facts described in paragraph (b) of this section, the date of the corporation's election (or intended election) under section 1362(a) or 1361(b)(3), a detailed explanation of the event or circumstance causing the termination or invalidity, when and how the event or circumstance was discovered, and the steps taken under paragraph (a)(3) of this section. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1368-1(g)(2)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1368-1#p-1.1368-1(g)(2)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1368-1(g)(2)(iii). An S corporation electing to treat its taxable year as separate taxable years after a qualifying disposition attaches a statement to its return that sets forth the facts relating to the qualifying disposition (sale, gift, stock issuance, or redemption) and states that the affected shareholders consent. | In the statement, the corporation must state that it is electing for the taxable year under § 1.1368-1(g)(2)(i) to treat the taxable year as if it consisted of separate taxable years. The corporation also must set forth facts in the statement relating to the qualifying disposition ( e.g. , sale, gift, stock issuance, or redemption), and state that each shareholder who held stock in the corporation during the taxable year (without regard to the election under § 1.1368-1(g)(2)(i)) consents to this election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1375-1(d)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1375-1#p-1.1375-1(d)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1375-1(d)(2). An S corporation requesting waiver of the passive investment income tax because it mistakenly believed it had no subchapter C earnings and profits submits a written request describing how and when both determinations were made and the steps taken to distribute the earnings, or a timetable for distribution with an explanation of why it is reasonable. | Such request shall contain a description of how and on what date the S corporation in good faith and using due diligence determined that it had no subchapter C earnings and profits at the close of the taxable year, a description of how and on what date it was determined that the S corporation had subchapter C earnings and profits at the close of the year and a description (including dates) of any steps taken to distribute such earnings and profits. If the earnings and profits have not yet been distributed, the request shall contain a timetable for distribution and an explanation of why such timetable is reasonable. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1441-1(e)(3)(iv)(D)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1441-1#p-1.1441-1(e)(3)(iv)(D)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1441-1(e)(3)(iv)(D)(5). A nonqualified intermediary that fails to cure a failure to provide withholding-rate-pool allocation information by February 14 may ask the IRS in writing to use the alternative procedures again, and the request must state why it did not comply and the steps taken or planned to prevent future failures. | Any nonqualified intermediary that fails to cure by February 14 may request the ability to use the alternative procedures of this paragraph (e)(3)(iv)(D) by submitting a request, in writing, to the IRS. The request must state the reason that the nonqualified intermediary did not comply with the alternative procedures of this paragraph (e)(3)(iv)(D) and steps that the nonqualified intermediary has taken, or will take, to ensure that no failures occur in the future. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(b)(4)(i)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-3#p-1.1445-3(b)(4)(i)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(b)(4)(i)(A). An application for a FIRPTA withholding certificate claiming reduced withholding must include a calculation of maximum tax supported by the contract and depreciation schedules, and if no depreciation schedules are provided, a statement of the nature of the property’s use and why depreciation was not allowable. | Such calculation must be accompanied by a copy of the relevant contract and depreciation schedules or other evidence that confirms the contract price and adjusted basis of the property. If no depreciation schedules are provided, the application must state the nature of the use of the property and why depreciation was not allowable. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(c)(3)(ii)(H)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-3#p-1.1445-3(c)(3)(ii)(H)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(c)(3). A foreign transferor applying for a FIRPTA withholding certificate on the ground that it has no unsatisfied withholding liability from its own acquisition of the property may submit, among the listed evidence, a signed statement under penalties of perjury setting forth the facts and circumstances supporting its conclusion that no withholding was required when it acquired the property. | (H) A statement, signed by the transferor under penalties of perjury, setting forth the facts and circumstances that supported the transferor's conclusion that no withholding was required under section 1445(a) with respect to the transferor's acquisition of the subject or predecessor real property interest. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(c)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-3#p-1.1445-3(c)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(c)(4). A transferor requesting a FIRPTA withholding certificate for a special reduction of withholding (because reduced withholding will not jeopardize collection) submits a statement of law and facts explaining why it cannot enter into an agreement for payment of tax under paragraph (e). | A transferor that requests a withholding certificate pursuant to this paragraph (c)(4) is required pursuant to paragraph (b)(4)(i)(C) of this section to submit a statement of law and facts in support of the request. That statement must explain why the transferor is unable to enter into an agreement for the payment of tax pursuant to paragraph (e) of this section. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(e)(3)(v)(B)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-3#p-1.1445-3(e)(3)(v)(B)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(e)(3)(v)(B). An application for a FIRPTA withholding certificate that proposes a non-preferred form of security must include a detailed statement of the facts and circumstances supporting that form of security and a memorandum of law on its validity and enforceability. | An application for a withholding certificate that proposes a form of security that does not conform with any of the preferred types set forth in paragraph (e)(3) (ii) through (iv) of this section or any relevant Revenue Procedure must include: ( 1 ) A detailed statement of the facts and circumstances supporting the use of the proposed form of security, and ( 2 ) A memorandum of law concerning the validity and enforceability of the proposed form of security. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3(f)(3)(iv)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-3#p-1.1445-3(f)(3)(iv)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-3, amending information (amending an application for a FIRPTA withholding certificate). An applicant who amends a pending application files an amending statement that fully sets forth the basis for the amendment, including any changed facts and any change sought in the certificate’s terms. | (iv) Amending information. The amending statement must fully set forth the basis for the amendment including any modification of the facts supporting the application for a withholding certificate and any change sought in the terms of the withholding certificate. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-5(c)(3)(v)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-5#p-1.1445-5(c)(3)(v)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-5(c)(3)(v). A partnership or trust that elected under paragraph (c)(3) to withhold upon distribution and wants to revoke that election applies for IRS consent with an application that repeats the election information, explains the reasons for revoking, and states the amount remaining to be distributed. | This application must include all information provided to the Service with the election notice and must provide an explanation of the reasons for revoking the election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-6(f)(3)(iv)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1445-6#p-1.1445-6(f)(3)(iv)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1445-6, amending information (amending an application for a section 1445(e) withholding certificate). An applicant who amends a pending application files an amending statement that fully sets forth the basis for the amendment, including any changed facts and any change sought in the certificate’s terms. | (iv) Amending information. The amending statement must fully set forth the basis for the amendment including any modification of the facts supporting the application for a withholding certificate and any change sought in the terms of the withholding certificate. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1446-3(b)(3)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1446-3#p-1.1446-3(b)(3)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1446-3(b)(3)(ii). A partnership that changes mid-year to the standard option annualization method for paying its section 1446 tax, after determining it will not qualify for the prior-year safe harbor, discloses the change in a statement attached to Form 8804 with information allowing the IRS to determine whether the change was appropriate. | A change pursuant to this paragraph shall be disclosed in a statement attached to the Form 8804 the partnership files for the taxable year and shall include information to allow the IRS to determine whether the change was appropriate. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1446-6(d)(3)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1446-6#p-1.1446-6(d)(3)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1446-6(d)(3)(ii). A partnership that failed to timely file a foreign partner’s certificate or attach the 1446 tax computation may obtain relief by showing reasonable cause and attaching the certificate, computation, and a written statement of the reasons for the failure to an amended Form 8813 or amended Forms 8804 and 8805. | To permit the partnership to reasonably rely on such certificate, the partnership shall be considered to have satisfied the requirements of paragraph (d)(3)(i) of this section if the partnership demonstrates that such failure was due to reasonable cause and not willful neglect and if once the partnership becomes aware of the failure, the partnership attaches the certificate and computation, as well as a written statement setting forth the reasons for the failure to comply with the requirements of paragraph (d)(3)(i) of this section, to an amended Form 8813 or amended Forms 8804 and 8805 for the relevant period. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1461-1(g)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1461-1#p-1.1461-1(g)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1461-1(g). A withholding agent requesting an extension of time to file Form 1042 (on Form 2758) or Form 1042-S (on Form 8809) includes a statement of the reasons for the request. | The request must contain a statement of the reasons for requesting the extension and such other information as the forms or instructions may require. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1471-4(d)(3)(vii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1471-4#p-1.1471-4(d)(3)(vii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1471-4(d)(3)(vii). A participating FFI seeking an additional 90-day hardship extension (beyond the automatic extension on Form 8809-I) to file Form 8966 includes a statement of the reasons for the request. | Under certain hardship conditions, the IRS may grant an additional 90-day extension. A request for extension due to hardship must contain a statement of the reasons for requesting the extension and such other information as the forms or instructions may require. | <sub>Treas. Reg. § 1.1474-1(i)(4)</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1471-4(g)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1471-4#p-1.1471-4(g)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1471-4 (FFI agreement), notice of event of default. A participating FFI that receives an IRS notice of default must respond, either providing the requested information or stating the reasons it disagrees that an event of default occurred. | The participating FFI must respond to the notice of default and provide information responsive to an IRS request for information or state the reasons why the participating FFI does not agree that an event of default has occurred. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1471-5(k)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1471-5#p-1.1471-5(k)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1471-5(k)(2) (sponsoring entities of sponsored FFIs). A sponsoring entity that receives an IRS notice of default must respond, either providing the requested information or stating the reasons it disagrees that an event of default occurred. | The sponsoring entity must respond to the notice of default and provide information responsive to an IRS request for information or state the reasons why the sponsoring entity does not agree that an event of default has occurred. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1472-1(c)(4)(vi)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1472-1#p-1.1472-1(c)(4)(vi)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1472-1(c)(4)(vi) (direct reporting NFFEs). A direct reporting NFFE that receives an IRS notice of default must respond, either providing the requested information or stating the reasons it disagrees that an event of default occurred. | The NFFE must respond to the notice of default and provide information responsive to an IRS request for information or state the reasons why the NFFE does not agree that an event of default has occurred. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1472-1(g)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1472-1#p-1.1472-1(g)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1472-1 (sponsoring entities of sponsored direct reporting NFFEs), notice of event of default. A sponsoring entity that receives an IRS notice of default must respond, either providing the requested information or stating the reasons it disagrees that an event of default occurred. | The sponsoring entity must respond to the notice of default and provide information responsive to an IRS request for information or state the reasons why the sponsoring entity does not agree that an event of default has occurred. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1502-68(c)(4)(ii)(B)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1502-68#p-1.1502-68(c)(4)(ii)(B)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1502-68(c)(4)(ii)(B). A transferee member or target electing not to have the consolidated acquisition rules apply attaches a statement to its return for the year beginning after the deconsolidation date describing the transaction(s) involved and stating that it is not claiming bonus depreciation for the property transferred. | A transferee member or target, as applicable, makes the election under this paragraph (c)(4) by attaching a statement to its return for the taxable year that begins on the day after the deconsolidation date. The statement must describe the transaction(s) to which the Consolidated Asset Acquisition Rule or Consolidated Deemed Acquisition Rule otherwise would apply and state that the transferee member or the target, as applicable, is not claiming the additional first year depreciation deduction for any eligible property transferred in such transaction(s). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.1503(d)-1(e)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.1503(d)-1#p-1.1503(d)-1(e)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.1503(d)-1(e)(2)(i) (reasonable cause relief for dual consolidated loss filings). A taxpayer that failed to timely file a required election, agreement, statement, rebuttal, computation, or other information attaches the missing documents to an amended return together with a written statement setting forth the reasons for the failure to timely comply. | Requests for reasonable cause relief will only be considered if once the person becomes aware of the failure to file the election, agreement, statement, rebuttal, computation or other information, the person attaches all the documents that should have been filed, as well as a written statement setting forth the reasons for the failure to timely comply, to an amended return that amends the return to which the documents should have been attached pursuant to the rules of this section and §§ 1.1503(d)-2 through 1.1503(d)-8. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.5000C-4(e)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.5000C-4#p-1.5000C-4(e)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.5000C-4(e) (refund of section 5000C tax overwithheld on federal procurement payments to foreign persons). A foreign contracting party claiming a refund files the required return stating the grounds on which the refund claim is based, and attaches a Section 5000C Certificate and a copy of the Form 1042-S from the acquiring agency. | In general, the contracting party making a refund claim must file the required return to claim a refund, stating the grounds upon which the claim is based. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.5000C-6, Example 5(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.5000C-6) | Treas. Reg. § 1.5000C-6, Example 5. A foreign contracting party that finds its estimate of nonexempt amounts on its Section 5000C Certificate was wrong and owes additional section 5000C tax files Form 1120-F reporting the liability and explains its corrected allocation method in the return. | Company C must explain its corrected allocation method in its Form 1120-F. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-1(j)(4)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038-1#p-1.6038-1(j)(4)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-1(j)(4)(ii) (information returns under section 6038 on foreign corporations). A domestic corporation seeking to avoid the foreign tax credit reduction for failing to furnish required information must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as reasonable cause in a written statement made under penalties of perjury. | (ii) A domestic corporation, which wishes to avoid a reduction in foreign tax credit as provided in subparagraphs (2) and (3) of this paragraph for failure to furnish information in accordance with this section, must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as a reasonable cause for such failure in the form of a written statement containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-2(k)(3)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038-2#p-1.6038-2(k)(3)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-2(k)(3)(ii). A U.S. person who failed to furnish required information about a controlled foreign corporation (Form 5471) and claims reasonable cause must file with the IRS a written statement, under penalties of perjury, affirmatively showing all facts alleged as reasonable cause. | To show that reasonable cause existed for failure to furnish information as required by section 6038 and this section, the person required to report such information must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as reasonable cause for such failure in a written statement containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of prejury. The statement must be filed with the district director for the district or the director of the service center where the return is required to be filed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-3(k)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038-3#p-1.6038-3(k)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-3(k)(4) (Form 8865 penalties for controlled foreign partnerships). A United States person seeking reasonable cause relief for failing to furnish required Form 8865 information provides a written statement to the IRS office having jurisdiction over its return, setting forth the reasons for the failure to comply. | The United States person may show reasonable cause by providing a written statement to the Commissioner's delegate having jurisdiction over the person's return to which the Form 8865 should have been attached, setting forth the reasons for the failure to comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-5(c)(3)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038-5#p-1.6038-5(c)(3)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038-5(c)(3)(ii) (section 6038 information reporting). A person claiming reasonable cause for failing to furnish required information files with the director a written statement, signed under penalties of perjury, affirmatively showing all facts alleged as reasonable cause for the failure. | (ii) To show that reasonable cause existed for failure to furnish information as required by section 6038 and this section, the person required to report such information must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as reasonable cause for such failure in a written statement containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury. The statement must be filed with the director where the return is required to be filed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038A-2(b)(1)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038A-2#p-1.6038A-2(b)(1)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038A-2(b)(1)(ii). A 25-percent foreign-owned U.S. reporting corporation filing Form 5472 identifies its direct and indirect foreign shareholders and, for an indirect 25-percent foreign shareholder, explains how ownership is attributed. | (ii) The name, address, and U.S. taxpayer identification number, if applicable, of all its direct and indirect foreign shareholders (for an indirect 25-percent foreign shareholder, explain the attribution of ownership); |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038A-4(b)(2)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038A-4#p-1.6038A-4(b)(2)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038A-4(b)(2)(i). A reporting corporation seeking reasonable-cause relief from the section 6038A monetary penalty (for failure to file Form 5472 or maintain records) must file a written statement, under penalties of perjury, affirmatively showing all facts alleged as reasonable cause. | To show that reasonable cause exists for purposes of paragraph (b)(1) of this section, the reporting corporation must make an affirmative showing of all the facts alleged as reasonable cause for the failure in a written statement containing a declaration that it is made under penalties of perjury. The statement must be filed with the District Director (in the case of failure to maintain or furnish requested information permitted to be maintained outside the United States within the time required under § 1.6038A-3(f) or a failure to file Form 5472) or the Director of the Internal Revenue Service Center where the Form 5472 is required to be filed (in the case of failure to file Form 5472). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1(c)(4)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038B-1#p-1.6038B-1(c)(4)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1(c)(4)(i). A U.S. person reporting a transfer of property to a foreign corporation describes property transferred for use in an active trade or business abroad, gives a general description of the transferee’s business, and, if a special active-business rule is relied on, provides information supporting its application. | (i) Active business property. Describe any transferred property that qualifies under § 1.367(a)-2(a)(2). Provide here a general description of the business conducted (or to be conducted) by the transferee, including the location of the business, the number of its employees, the nature of the business, and copies of the most recently prepared balance sheet and profit and loss statement. … If property is considered to be transferred for use in the active conduct of a trade or business under a special rule in paragraph (e), (f), or (g) of § 1.367(a)-2, specify the applicable rule and provide information supporting the application of the rule. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1(f)(3)(ii)(A)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038B-1#p-1.6038B-1(f)(3)(ii)(A)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1(f)(3)(ii)(A) (reporting of transfers to foreign corporations). A U.S. transferor seeking reasonable cause relief for failing to timely comply files an amended return that includes the missing information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to timely comply. | A U.S. transferor's statement that the failure to timely comply was due to reasonable cause and not willful neglect will be considered only if, promptly after the U.S. transferor becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to timely comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1T(c)(5)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038B-1T#p-1.6038B-1T(c)(5)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1T(c)(5)(i). A U.S. person reporting a transfer of the property of a foreign branch to a foreign corporation describes the foreign branch whose property is transferred, as defined in § 1.367(a)-6T(g). | (i) Branch operation. Describe the foreign branch the property of which is transferred, in accordance with the definition of § 1.367(a)-6T(g). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1T(d)(1)(v)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038B-1T#p-1.6038B-1T(d)(1)(v)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1T(d)(1)(v). A U.S. person reporting a transfer of intangible property to a foreign corporation subject to section 367(d) provides and explains the calculation of the annual deemed payment it must recognize for use of the intangible. | (v) Annual payment. Provide and explain the calculation of the annual deemed payment for the use of the intangible property required to be recognized by the transferor under the rules of section 367(d). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1T(d)(1)(viii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038B-1T#p-1.6038B-1T(d)(1)(viii)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-1T(d)(1)(viii). A U.S. person reporting a section 367(d) transfer of intangibles to a foreign corporation also describes any intangible property it sold or licensed to the transferee foreign corporation and sets forth the general terms of each sale or license. | (viii) Other intangibles. Describe any intangible property sold or licensed by the transferor to the transferee foreign corporation, and set forth the general terms of each sale or license. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-2(h)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6038B-2#p-1.6038B-2(h)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6038B-2(h)(3) (reporting of transfers to foreign partnerships). A United States person seeking reasonable cause relief for failing to comply files an amended return that includes the missing information and a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to comply. | A United States person's statement that the failure to comply was due to reasonable cause and not willful neglect will be considered timely only if, promptly after the United States person becomes aware of the failure, an amended return is filed for the taxable year to which the failure relates that includes the information that should have been included with the original return for such taxable year or that otherwise complies with the rules of this section, and that includes a written statement explaining the reasons for the failure to comply. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6049-3(c)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6049-3#p-1.6049-3(c)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6049-3(c)(2) (statements to recipients of interest and OID). A payer seeking up to a 30-day extension to furnish recipient statements files a written application with the district director containing a full recital of the reasons for requesting the extension. | The application shall be addressed to the district director with whom the income tax returns of the applicant are filed and shall contain a full recital of the reasons for requesting the extension to aid the district director in determining the period of the extension, if any, which will be granted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6050E-1(l)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6050E-1#p-1.6050E-1(l)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6050E-1(l)(2) (statements for State and local income tax refunds). A refund officer seeking up to a 30-day extension to furnish recipient statements files a written application with the Service Center containing a concise statement of the reasons for requesting the extension. | The application shall be addressed to the Service Center with which the Forms 1099 required under this section are required to be filed and shall contain a concise statement of the reasons for requesting the extension to aid the service center director in determining the period of the extension, if any, which will be granted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6050J-1T(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6050J-1T#p-1.6050J-1T(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6050J-1T, Q&A-42 (statements to borrowers on foreclosures and abandonments). A lender seeking up to a 30-day extension to furnish borrower statements files a written application with the service center containing a concise statement of the reasons for requesting the extension. | The application must contain a concise statement of the reasons for requesting the extension in order to aid the service center director in determining the period of extension, if any, to be granted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6081-1(b)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6081-1#p-1.6081-1(b)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6081-1, taxpayer unable to sign (extension of time to file returns). If the taxpayer cannot sign an extension request because of illness, absence, or other good cause and a person in a close personal or business relationship signs instead, the request must state the reasons for the substitute signature and the relationship between the taxpayer and the signer. | In any case in which a taxpayer is unable, by reason of illness, absence, or other good cause, to sign a request for an extension, any person standing in close personal or business relationship to the taxpayer may sign the request on his behalf, and shall be considered as a duly authorized agent for this purpose, provided the request sets forth the reasons for a signature other than the taxpayer's and the relationship existing between the taxpayer and the signer. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 1.6695-1(b)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-1.6695-1#p-1.6695-1(b)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 1.6695-1(b)(3) (penalty on a tax return preparer who fails to sign a return or refund claim). If the preparer asserts reasonable cause for the failure to sign, the preparer must give the IRS a written statement substantiating the claim of reasonable cause. | If the tax return preparer asserts reasonable cause for failure to sign, the IRS will require a written statement to substantiate the tax return preparer's claim of reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 6a.6652(g)-1(e)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-6a.6652(g)-1#p-6a.6652(g)-1(e)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 6a.6652(g)-1(e)(2) (penalty for failing to make a section 6039C return or furnish a statement on foreign investment in U.S. real property). A person claiming reasonable cause must submit a written statement, made under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement, made under the penalties of perjury, containing a declaration by the person failing to make a return or furnish a statement under section 6039C setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 20.2031-7(d)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-20.2031-7#p-20.2031-7(d)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 20.2031-7 (estate tax valuation of annuities, life and term interests, and remainders; special factors). An executor who requests an IRS ruling for a special actuarial factor must accompany the request with a recitation of the facts, including each measuring life’s date of birth, the date of death, other applicable dates, and copies of the will, trust, or other relevant documents. | The request for a ruling must be accompanied by a recitation of the facts including a statement of the date of birth for each measuring life, the date of the decedent's death, any other applicable dates, and a copy of the will, trust, or other relevant documents. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 20.2053-1(d)(5)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-20.2053-1#p-20.2053-1(d)(5)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 20.2053-1(d)(5)(i). An estate filing a protective claim for refund to preserve a deduction for a claim or expense not yet paid must identify each outstanding claim or expense and describe the reasons and contingencies delaying its actual payment. | Although the protective claim need not state a particular dollar amount or demand an immediate refund, a protective claim must identify each outstanding claim or expense that would have been deductible under section 2053(a) or (b) if such item already had been paid and must describe the reasons and contingencies delaying the actual payment of the claim or expense. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2011-48, section 4.05(1), 2011-42 I.R.B. 527, at 529</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 20.2106-2(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-20.2106-2#p-20.2106-2(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 20.2106-2(b). The executor of a nonresident alien decedent’s estate claiming deductions for expenses, claims, and liabilities that were not included in a foreign death-duty or foreign-court schedule submits a written statement of the foreign executor, under penalties of perjury, setting forth the facts relied on as entitling the estate to each deduction. | If the items of deduction allowable under section 2106(a)(1) were not included in either such schedule, or if no such schedules were filed, then there should be submitted a written statement of the foreign executor containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury setting forth the facts relied upon as entitling the estate to the benefit of the particular deduction or deductions. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 20.6018-4(g)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-20.6018-4#p-20.6018-4(g)) | Treas. Reg. § 20.6018-4(g) (documents to accompany the estate tax return). If the executor contends that property transferred within three years of death should be excluded from the gross estate because the transfer was not made in contemplation of death, the executor files with the return a copy of the death certificate and a statement, under penalties of perjury, of all material facts and circumstances, including the decedent’s motive for the transfer and mental and physical condition at the time. | (g) If the executor contends that the value of property transferred by the decedent within a period of three years ending with the date of the decedent's death should not be included in the gross estate because he considers that the transfer was not made in contemplation of death, he shall file with the return (1) a copy of the death certificate, and (2) a statement, continuing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury, of all the material facts and circumstances, including those directly or indirectly indicating the decedent's motive in making the transfer and his mental and physical condition at that time. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 20.6081-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-20.6081-1#p-20.6081-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 20.6081-1(c). An executor requesting a discretionary extension of time to file Form 706 for good cause files Form 4768 with a detailed explanation of why it is impossible or impractical to file a reasonably complete return by the due date. | To obtain such an extension, Form 4768 must be filed in accordance with the procedures under paragraph (a) of this section and must contain a detailed explanation of why it is impossible or impractical to file a reasonably complete return by the due date. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 20.6081-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-20.6081-1#p-20.6081-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 20.6081-1(c). If the estate did not request an automatic extension of time to file Form 706, the Form 4768 requesting a discretionary extension must also contain an explanation showing good cause for not requesting the automatic extension. | If an estate did not request an automatic extension of time to file Form 706 under paragraph (b) of this section, Form 4768 must also contain an explanation showing good cause for not requesting the automatic extension. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 25.2512-5(d)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-25.2512-5#p-25.2512-5(d)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 25.2512-5 (gift tax valuation of annuities, unitrust, life and term interests, and remainders; special factors). A donor who requests an IRS ruling for a special actuarial factor must accompany the request with a recitation of the facts, including each measuring life’s date of birth, the date of the gift, other applicable dates, and copies of the will, trust, or other relevant documents. | The request for a ruling must be accompanied by a recitation of the facts including a statement of the date of birth for each measuring life, the date of the gift, any other applicable dates, and a copy of the will, trust, or other relevant documents. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 25.6019-1(h)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-25.6019-1#p-25.6019-1(h)) | Treas. Reg. § 25.6019-1(h) (gift tax return made by an agent). If illness, absence, or nonresidence prevents the donor from making the return and an agent signs it, the return must be accompanied by a statement fully explaining the donor’s inability to make it. | If a return is signed by an agent, a statement fully explaining the inability of the donor must accompany the return. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 26.2601-1(b)(3)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-26.2601-1#p-26.2601-1(b)(3)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 26.2601-1(b)(3)(iii)(A). To use the GST grandfather rule for a decedent under a mental disability since October 22, 1986, who was never adjudged incompetent, the executor files with Form 706 either a physician’s certification or other sufficient evidence of incompetence together with a statement explaining why no physician’s certification is available. | If there has not been a court adjudication that the decedent was mentally incompetent on or before October 22, 1986, the executor must file, with Form 706, either— ( 1 ) A certification from a qualified physician stating that the decedent was— ( i ) Mentally incompetent at all times on and after October 22, 1986; and ( ii ) Did not regain competence to modify or revoke the terms of the trust or will prior to his or her death; or ( 2 ) Sufficient other evidence demonstrating that the decedent was mentally incompetent at all times on and after October 22, 1986, as well as a statement explaining why no certification is available from a physician; and ( 3 ) Any judgement or decree relating to the decedent's incompetency that was made after October 22, 1986. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 26.2642-7(i)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-26.2642-7#p-26.2642-7(i)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 26.2642-7(i)(3)(i). A transferor or executor requesting section 2642(g)(1) relief (through a private letter ruling) for a late GST exemption allocation or election must submit a detailed affidavit describing the events that led to the failure and its discovery and, if a tax professional was relied on, the scope of the engagement, the professional’s responsibilities, and the extent of reliance. | The transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate must submit a detailed affidavit describing the events that led to the failure to timely allocate GST exemption to a transfer or the failure to timely elect under section 2632(b)(3) or (c)(5), and the events that led to the discovery of the failure. In situations described in paragraph (i)(4)(vi) of this section, this affidavit also must include the additional information and statements described in that paragraph. If the transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate relied on a tax professional for advice with respect to the allocation or election, the affidavit also must describe— (A) The scope of the engagement; (B) The responsibilities the transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate believed the professional had assumed; and (C) The extent to which the transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate relied on the professional. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 26.2642-7(i)(4)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-26.2642-7#p-26.2642-7(i)(4)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 26.2642-7(i)(4). With a request for section 2642(g)(1) relief, the transferor or executor must also submit detailed affidavits from specified individuals and others with knowledge of the events leading to the failure to allocate GST exemption or make the election (or its discovery), each describing the individual’s engagement, responsibilities, and advice or service provided. | The transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate must submit detailed affidavits from the individuals specified in paragraphs (i)(4)(i)(A) through (D) of this section and other individuals who have knowledge or information about the events that led to the failure to allocate GST exemption or to elect under section 2632(b)(3) or (c)(5), or to the discovery of the failure. … Each affidavit must describe the scope of the engagement and the responsibilities of the individual as well as the advice or service the individual provided to the transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 26.2642-7(i)(4)(vi)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-26.2642-7#p-26.2642-7(i)(4)(vi)) | Treas. Reg. § 26.2642-7(i)(4)(vi) (section 2642(g)(1) relief for late GST exemption allocations or elections). If an individual required to provide an affidavit has died or is unwilling or unable to do so, the transferor’s or executor’s affidavit must say so and describe that individual’s relationship to the transferor or executor, what that individual is believed to have known about the failure, and, except in the case of death, the efforts made to obtain the affidavit. | (vi) If an individual who would be required to provide an affidavit under paragraph (i)(4)(i) of this section has died or is unwilling or otherwise unable to provide the required affidavit, the affidavit required under paragraph (i)(3) of this section must include a statement to that effect, as well as a statement describing the relationship between that individual and the transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate; the information or knowledge the transferor or the executor of the transferor's estate believes that individual had about the events that led to the failure to make the allocation or the election or to the discovery of that failure; and, in cases other than the death of the individual, a detailed description of the efforts made to obtain the affidavit from the individual. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 26.2654-1(b)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-26.2654-1#p-26.2654-1(b)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 26.2654-1(b)(2). If a court order severing a trust for GST purposes has not been issued when the estate tax return is filed, the executor attaches a statement indicating that a severance proceeding has begun and describing the manner in which the trust is proposed to be severed, along with a copy of the petition. | If a court order severing the trust has not been issued at the time the Federal estate tax return is filed, the executor must indicate on a statement attached to the return that a proceeding has been commenced to sever the trust and describe the manner in which the trust is proposed to be severed. A copy of the petition or other instrument used to commence the proceeding must also be attached to the return. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 31.3102-3(c)(2)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-31.3102-3#p-31.3102-3(c)(2)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 31.3102-3, reasonableness of estimate (employer collecting employee FICA tax on tips on the basis of estimated tips). An employer using estimates of the tips employees will report must be prepared, on request of the district director, to disclose the factors relied on in making the estimate and its reasons for believing the estimate is reasonable. | The employer must be prepared, upon request of the district director, to disclose the factors upon which he relied in making the estimate, and his reasons for believing that the estimate is reasonable. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 31.3402(h)(1)-1(b)(2)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-31.3402(h)(1)-1#p-31.3402(h)(1)-1(b)(2)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 31.3402(h)(1)-1, reasonableness of estimate (income tax withholding on the basis of estimated tips). An employer that withholds on estimated tips must be prepared, on request of the district director, to disclose the factors relied on in making the estimate and its reasons for believing the estimate is reasonable. | The employer must be prepared, upon request of the district director, to disclose the factors upon which he relied in making the estimate, and his reasons for believing that the estimate is reasonable. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 31.6051-1(d)(2)(i)(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-31.6051-1#p-31.6051-1(d)(2)(i)(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 31.6051-1 (extension of time to furnish Forms W-2 to employees). If illness, absence, or other good cause prevents the employer from signing the extension request and a person in a close personal or business relationship signs instead, the request must state a reason for the substitute signature and the relationship between the employer and the signer. | In any case in which an employer is unable, by reason of illness, absence, or other good cause, to sign a request for an extension, any person standing in close personal or business relationship to the employer may sign the request on his behalf, and shall be considered as a duly authorized agent for this purpose, provided the request sets forth a reason for a signature other than the employer's and the relationship existing between the employer and the signer. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 31.6081(a)-1(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-31.6081(a)-1#p-31.6081(a)-1(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 31.6081(a)-1(b) (extension of time to file the FUTA return, Form 940). An employer seeking an extension files a written application with the IRS officer with whom the return will be filed, containing a full recital of the reasons for requesting the extension. | Each application shall contain a full recital of the reasons for requesting the extension, to aid such officer in determining the period of the extension, if any, which will be granted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 31.6081(a)-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-31.6081(a)-1#p-31.6081(a)-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 31.6081(a)-1(c) (extension of time to file employment tax returns). If the employer cannot sign the extension request because of illness, absence, or other good cause, a person in close personal or business relationship may sign it, provided the request states why someone other than the employer signed and the signer’s relationship to the employer. | In any case in which an employer is unable, by reason of illness, absence, or other good cause, to sign a request for an extension, any person standing in close personal or business relationship to the employer may sign the request on his behalf, and shall be considered as a duly authorized agent for this purpose, provided the requests sets forth the reasons for a signature other than the employer's and the relationship existing between the employer and the signer. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 35.3405-1T(e)(2), (e)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-35.3405-1T#p-35.3405-1T(e)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 35.3405-1T, Q&A g-8 and g-9. A pension payor or plan administrator requesting an additional delay (to July 1, 1983) in implementing pension withholding for undue hardship files a statement under penalties of perjury giving a complete statement of the facts showing why the delay is warranted, an implementation schedule, and an explanation of its good-faith steps to comply. | (2) A complete statement of the facts upon which the payor is relying to show why a delay beyond April 1, 1983, is warranted. … (4) An explanation of the steps taken which demonstrate the payor's or plan administrator's good faith attempt to comply with these notice and withholding requirements. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 35a.3406-2(k)(2)(ii)(C)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-35a.3406-2#p-35a.3406-2(k)(2)(ii)(C)) | Treas. Reg. § 35a.3406-2(k)(2)(ii)(C). A payee seeking waiver of the section 6682 civil penalty for a false certification that it was not subject to backup withholding submits to the IRS a signed written statement, with supporting documents, establishing all the facts necessary to obtain the waiver. | To request a waiver under section 6682(b) and paragraph (k)(2)(ii)(B) of this section, the payee must submit to the Internal Revenue Service a written statement with supporting documents to establish all the facts necessary in order to obtain the waiver. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 36.3121(l)(7)-1(a)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-36.3121(l)(7)-1#p-36.3121(l)(7)-1(a)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 36.3121(l)(7)-1. A domestic corporation that reports a correction of amounts owed under its section 3121(l) agreement covering employees of foreign affiliates attaches to the return a statement explaining the error being corrected and designating the quarter in which it was ascertained. | Every return on which such a correction (by adjustment, credit, or otherwise) is reported pursuant to this section must have securely attached as a part thereof a statement explaining the error in respect of which the correction is made, designating the calendar quarter in which the error was ascertained, and setting forth such other information as would be required if the correction were in respect of an overpayment or underpayment of taxes under the Federal Insurance Contributions Act. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 44.6419-1(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-44.6419-1#p-44.6419-1(a)) | Treas. Reg. § 44.6419-1(a) (overpayment of the section 4401 wagering excise tax). A person who overpays the tax and claims a refund on Form 843 or a credit on a later monthly return must attach a complete statement of the facts involving the overpayment to the claim or the return. | If a person overpays the tax imposed under section 4401, he may either file a claim for refund on Form 843 or take credit for such overpayment against the tax due on a subsequent monthly return. A complete statement of the facts involving the overpayment shall be attached either to the claim or to the return on which the credit is claimed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 44.6419-1(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-44.6419-1#p-44.6419-1(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 44.6419-1(b). A person claiming a credit or refund of overpaid wagering tax must file a statement satisfactorily explaining the reason for the claim and establishing that the tax was not collected from the bettor, or was repaid to the bettor or the bettor consented to the refund. | No credit or refund shall be allowed whether in pursuance of a court decision or otherwise unless the taxpayer files a statement explaining satisfactorily the reason for claiming the credit or refund and establishing (1) that he has not collected (whether as a separate charge or otherwise) the amount of the tax from the person who placed the wager on which the tax was imposed, or (2) that he has either repaid the amount of the tax to the person who placed the wager or has secured the written consent of such person to the allowance of the credit or refund. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 44.6419-2(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-44.6419-2#p-44.6419-2(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 44.6419-2(b) (refund of wagering tax paid on laid-off wagers). A taxpayer claiming a refund on Form 843 attaches a statement setting forth the reason for claiming the refund, the month and date the tax was paid, and whether any earlier refund claim covering the amount has been filed. | If a refund is claimed, Form 843 shall be completed in accordance with the instructions thereon and, in addition, there shall be attached to such form a statement setting forth the reason for claiming the refund, the month in which such tax was paid, the date of payment, and whether any previous claim for refund covering the amount involved or any part thereof has been filed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 48.6412-1(f)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-48.6412-1#p-48.6412-1(f)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 48.6412-1(f)(2) (manufacturer’s claim for floor stocks credit or refund of manufacturers excise tax). A manufacturer claiming the credit or refund submits a signed statement describing in general terms the articles covered, setting forth the method used to compute the amount claimed (including any procedures used under § 48.6412-3), and making the listed certifications. | No credit or refund shall be allowed under this section unless there is submitted, in support of the claim for credit or refund, a statement signed by the person making the claim, that describes in general terms the articles covered by the claim, sets forth the method of computing the amount claimed (including a description of any procedures used pursuant to § 48.6412-3), and states that— (i) The claimant paid to the district director or the director of the internal revenue service center the tax for which credit or refund is claimed; (ii) The total amount claimed represents payments requested by dealers before the dealer request limitation date; (iii) The total amount claimed either was paid by the claimant to the dealers, or the claimant received the written consent of the dealers to the allowance of the amount claimed: (iv) The claimant has in his possession, and available for inspection by internal revenue officers, the evidence with respect to inventories required by paragraph (g)(2) of this section, and any written consents referred to in paragraph (f)(2)(iii) of this section; and (v) No other claim for credit or refund under this section has been or will be made by the claimant with respect to any amount covered by the claim. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 53.4942(a)-3(b)(7)(ii)(e)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-53.4942(a)-3#p-53.4942(a)-3(b)(7)(ii)(e)) | Treas. Reg. § 53.4942(a)-3(b)(7)(ii). A private foundation seeking approval of a set-aside under the cash distribution test must submit statements including a statement of actual qualifying payments; that statement should describe in detail any payments to be treated as distributed in an earlier year under the special start-up rules and explain the circumstances that justify applying those rules. | This statement should include a detailed description of any payments that are to be treated, pursuant to the rules of subparagraphs (4)(iv) and (6)(ii) of this paragraph (b), as distributed during a taxable year prior to the taxable year in which such payments were actually made and, in addition, should explain the circumstances that justify the application of those rules. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 53.4945-4(d)(1)(i)–(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-53.4945-4#p-53.4945-4(d)(1)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 53.4945-4(d)(1)(i)–(iii). The foundation’s request for advance approval of individual grant procedures must include a statement describing the selection process in enough detail to show the grants are objective and nondiscriminatory, a description of the terms and conditions of the grants, and a detailed description of the foundation’s procedure for supervising grants. | The request must contain the following items: (i) A statement describing the selection process. Such statement shall be sufficiently detailed for the Commissioner to determine whether the grants are made on an objective and nondiscriminatory basis under paragraph (b) of this section. (ii) A description of the terms and conditions under which the foundation ordinarily makes such grants, which is sufficient to enable the Commissioner to determine whether the grants awarded under such procedures would meet the requirements of paragraph (1), (2), or (3) of section 4945(g). (iii) A detailed description of the private foundation's procedure for exercising supervision over grants, as described in paragraph (c) (2) and (3) of this section. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6104(a)-5(a)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6104(a)-5#p-301.6104(a)-5(a)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6104(a)-5(a)(1). An exempt organization whose application for exemption is open to public inspection may ask in writing that information about trade secrets, patents, processes, styles of work, or apparatus be withheld; the request must identify the material precisely and state why it should not be open to public inspection. | The request must clearly identify the material desired to be withheld (the document, page, paragraph, and line) and must state why the information should not be open to public inspection. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6104(d)-3(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6104(d)-3#p-301.6104(d)-3(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6104(d)-3(d) (tax-exempt organization subject to a harassment campaign). An organization seeking a determination that it may disregard document requests that are part of a harassment campaign submits a signed written application describing in detail the facts and circumstances that support that determination. | The application shall consist of a written statement giving the organization's name, address, employer identification number, and the name, address and telephone number of the person to contact regarding the application. The application must describe in detail the facts and circumstances that the organization believes support a determination that the organization is subject to a harassment campaign. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6110-5(c)(2)(ii)(C)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6110-5#p-301.6110-5(c)(2)(ii)(C)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6110-5(c)(2)(ii) (postponing public inspection of a written determination because the underlying transaction is incomplete). The person to whom the determination pertains may make a written request to delay disclosure until after the transaction is scheduled to be completed; under paragraph (c)(2)(ii)(C) the request must give the expected completion date and, for an additional extension, the reason for it. | The written request for extension of the time when a written determination is to be made open to public inspection or available for inspection upon written request shall set forth the date on which it is expected that the underlying transaction will be completed, and, with respect to the additional extension described in paragraph (c)(2)(ii)(B) of this section, set forth the reason for requesting such extension. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6222-1(d)(2)(iii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6222-1#p-301.6222-1(d)(2)(iii)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6222-1(d)(2)(iii) (partner’s election to be treated as having notified the IRS of an inconsistent treatment of a partnership-related item). If the partnership’s statement, schedule, or other form does not make clear that the partner’s return treatment is consistent, the election must include an explanation of how the two treatments are consistent, including as to characterization, timing, and amount. | However, if it is not clear from the statement, schedule, or other form furnished by the partnership that the partner's treatment of the partnership-related item on the partner's return is consistent, the election must also include an explanation of how the treatment of such item on the statement, schedule, or other form furnished by the partnership is consistent with the treatment of the item on the partner's return, including with respect to the characterization, timing, and amount of such item. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6227-2(a)(2)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6227-2#p-301.6227-2(a)(2)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6227-2(a)(2)(ii). A partnership that applies modifications to an imputed underpayment resulting from an administrative adjustment request (AAR) must, as part of the AAR, describe the effect of each modification and explain the basis for it, along with supporting documentation. | As part of the AAR filed with the IRS in accordance with forms, instructions, and other guidance prescribed by the IRS, the partnership must— (A) Notify the IRS of any modification; (B) Describe the effect of the modification on the imputed underpayment; (C) Provide an explanation of the basis for such modification; and (D) Provide documentation to support the partnership's eligibility for the modification. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6320-1(c)(2)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6320-1#p-301.6320-1(c)(2)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6320-1(c)(2), Q&A-C1(ii). A taxpayer’s written request for a Collection Due Process hearing on the filing of a notice of federal tax lien must state the reason or reasons the taxpayer disagrees with the filing; an incomplete request must be perfected after IRS contact. | (ii) The written request for a CDP hearing must be dated and must include the following: (A) The taxpayer's name, address, daytime telephone number (if any), and taxpayer identification number (e.g., SSN, ITIN or EIN). (B) The type of tax involved. (C) The tax period at issue. (D) A statement that the taxpayer requests a hearing with Appeals concerning the filing of the NFTL. (E) The reason or reasons why the taxpayer disagrees with the filing of the NFTL. (F) The signature of the taxpayer or the taxpayer's authorized representative. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6320-1(i)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6320-1#p-301.6320-1(i)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6320-1(i)(2), Q&A-I1. A taxpayer’s written request for an equivalent hearing with Appeals concerning a notice of federal tax lien must state the reason or reasons the taxpayer disagrees with the filing, and a deficient request must be perfected or it will be denied. | (ii) The request must be dated and must include the following: (A) The taxpayer's name, address, daytime telephone number (if any), and taxpayer identification number (e.g., SSN, ITIN or EIN). (B) The type of tax involved. (C) The tax period at issue. (D) A statement that the taxpayer is requesting an equivalent hearing with Appeals concerning the filing of the NFTL. (E) The reason or reasons why the taxpayer disagrees with the filing of the NFTL. (F) The signature of the taxpayer or the taxpayer's authorized representative. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6323(i)-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6323(i)-1#p-301.6323(i)-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6323(i)-1. A person with a proper interest who asks the IRS office named on a notice of federal tax lien for the amount still owed must describe the property subject to the lien, identify the lien, and give the reasons for requesting the information. | The request should clearly describe the property subject to the lien, identify the applicable lien, and give the reasons for requesting the information. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6325-1(c)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6325-1#p-301.6325-1(c)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6325-1(c)(2) (application for a certificate discharging property from the estate or gift tax lien). The written application, made under penalties of perjury, should explain the circumstances requiring the discharge and fully describe the items to be discharged; if the estate or gift tax return has not been filed, a statement may be required showing the value of the property, the basis for that valuation, and related information. | It should be made in writing under penalties of perjury and should explain the circumstances that require the discharge, and should fully describe the particular items for which the discharge is desired. … If the estate or gift tax return has not been filed, a statement under penalties of perjury may be required showing (i) the value of the property to be discharged, (ii) the basis for such valuation, (iii) in the case of the estate tax, the approximate value of the gross estate and the approximate value of the total real property included in the gross estate, (iv) in the case of the gift tax, the total amount of gifts made during the calendar year and the prior calendar years subsequent to the enactment of the Revenue Act of 1932 and the approximate value of all real estate subject to the gift tax lien, and (v) if the property is to be sold or otherwise transferred, the name and address of the purchaser or transferee and the consideration, if any, paid or to be paid by him. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6326-1(d)(2)(iii)(B)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6326-1#p-301.6326-1(d)(2)(iii)(B)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6326-1(d)(2)(iii)(B). A person administratively appealing the filing of a notice of federal tax lien on the ground that the underlying liability was assessed in violation of the deficiency procedures must explain in the appeal how the assessment was erroneous. | (B) If the ground upon which the filing of notice is being appealed is that the tax liability that gave rise to lien was assessed in violation of the deficiency procedures set forth in section 6213 of the Internal Revenue Code, the appealing party must explain how the assessment was erroneous. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6343-1(d)(1)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6343-1#p-301.6343-1(d)(1)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6343-1(d)(1)(i). A taxpayer seeking an expedited determination on release of a levy on essential business property must submit the required release-request information together with an explanation of why the levied property qualifies for an expedited determination. | In order to obtain an expedited determination, the taxpayer must submit, within the time frame specified in paragraph (c)(2) of this section, the information required in paragraph (c)(1) of this section and include with the information an explanation of why the property levied upon qualifies for an expedited determination of whether a condition requiring release of the levy exists. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6402-2(b)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6402-2#p-301.6402-2(b)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6402-2(b)(1) (claims for credit or refund). A refund claim must set forth in detail each ground on which the credit or refund is claimed and facts sufficient to apprise the IRS of its exact basis, verified under penalties of perjury. | The claim must set forth in detail each ground upon which a credit or refund is claimed and facts sufficient to apprise the Commissioner of the exact basis thereof. The statement of the grounds and facts must be verified by a written declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2011-48, section 4.02, 2011-42 I.R.B. 527, at 528</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6501(c)-1(f)(2)(v)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6501(c)-1#p-301.6501(c)-1(f)(2)(v)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6501(c)-1(f)(2)(v). For a gift to be adequately disclosed on a gift tax return (starting the assessment period), the donor must include a statement describing any position taken that is contrary to proposed, temporary, or final regulations or revenue rulings published at the time of the transfer. | (v) A statement describing any position taken that is contrary to any proposed, temporary or final Treasury regulations or revenue rulings published at the time of the transfer (see § 601.601(d)(2) of this chapter). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6651-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6651-1#p-301.6651-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6651-1(c)(1). A taxpayer seeking to avoid the failure-to-file or failure-to-pay addition to tax must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as reasonable cause in a written statement made under penalties of perjury, filed with the appropriate IRS office. | (1) Except as provided in subparagraphs (3) and (4) of this paragraph (b), a taxpayer who wishes to avoid the addition to the tax for failure to file a tax return or pay tax must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as a reasonable cause for his failure to file such return or pay such tax on time in the form of a written statement containing a declaration that it is made under penalties of perjury. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6652-1(f)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6652-1#p-301.6652-1(f)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6652-1(f) (penalty for failure to file certain information returns). A person seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause must submit a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement, containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as a reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6652-2(f)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6652-2#p-301.6652-2(f)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6652-2(f) (penalty on exempt organizations for failure to file annual returns or comply with public inspection rules). An organization claiming reasonable cause must submit a written statement, declared under penalties of perjury by the responsible person or an officer, director, or trustee, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement containing a declaration by the appropriate person (as defined in paragraph (d)(1) of this section), or in his absence, by any officer, director, or trustee of the organization, that the statement is made under the penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6652-3(b)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6652-3#p-301.6652-3(b)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6652-3(b)(3). A plan administrator, trustee, custodian, or issuer seeking relief from the section 6652(e) or (f) penalty for failing to file plan information returns or statements (e.g., under sections 6057, 6058, or 6047) must submit a written statement under penalties of perjury setting forth all facts alleged as reasonable cause. | (3) An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. The statement must contain a declaration by the appropriate individual that the statement is made under the penalties of perjury. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6678-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6678-1#p-301.6678-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6678-1(c) (penalty for failure to furnish statements to payees). A person seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause must submit a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement, containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as a reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6679-1(a)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6679-1#p-301.6679-1(a)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6679-1(a)(3) (penalty for failure to file returns on foreign corporations or foreign partnerships under sections 6035, 6046, and 6046A). A taxpayer seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause must submit a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement, containing a declaration that it is made under the penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as a reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6686-1(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6686-1#p-301.6686-1(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6686-1(b) (penalty on a DISC for failure to file returns or supply information). A person seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause must submit a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement, which contains a declaration by such person that the statement is made under the penalties of perjury, and sets forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6688-1(c)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6688-1#p-301.6688-1(c)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6688-1(c)(1) (penalty for failure to file possessions information returns, including Form 8898). An individual seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause files a written statement, under penalties of perjury, with the service center where Form 8898 is filed, affirmatively showing all facts alleged as reasonable cause for the late filing. | An individual who wishes to avoid the penalty must make an affirmative showing of all facts alleged as a reasonable cause for failure to file the information return on time, or furnish the information on time, in the form of a written statement containing a declaration that it is made under penalties of perjury. This statement must be filed with Internal Revenue Service Center where Form 8898 must be filed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6689-1(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6689-1#p-301.6689-1(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6689-1(d) (penalty for failure to notify the IRS of a foreign tax redetermination). A taxpayer seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause files with the service center a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause for the late notification. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement that sets forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause for the failure to file the notification on time and that contains a declaration by the taxpayer that the statement is made under the penalties of perjury. This statement must be filed with the Internal Revenue Service Center in which the notification was required to be filed. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6692-1(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6692-1#p-301.6692-1(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6692-1(c) (penalty for failure to file a pension plan actuarial report). A plan administrator seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause must submit a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement setting forth all the facts alleged as reasonable cause. The statement must contain a declaration by the appropriate individual that the statement is made under the penalties of perjury. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6693-1(b)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6693-1#p-301.6693-1(b)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6693-1(b) (penalty for failure to provide IRA reports and documents).
