A person seeking relief on the ground of reasonable cause must submit a written statement, under penalties of perjury, setting forth all the facts alleged to constitute reasonable cause. | An affirmative showing of reasonable cause must be made in the form of a written statement, containing a declaration by such person that it is made under the penalties of perjury and setting forth all the facts alleged to constitute reasonable cause. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6708-1(c)(3)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6708-1#p-301.6708-1(c)(3)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6708-1(c)(3)(ii) (extension of the 20-business-day period to produce a material advisor’s list of advisees). A person requesting an extension must apply in writing to the IRS employee who requested the list, briefly describing the information and documents in the list, explaining the circumstances that warrant more time, proposing a production schedule, and making the required statements about list maintenance. | Any request for an extension of the 20-business-day period must be made in writing to the person at the IRS who requested the list. The person requesting an extension must briefly describe the information and documents that comprise the list as required by section 6112; explain the circumstances that would warrant additional time; propose a schedule to complete the production of the list; state that to the best of the person's knowledge, as of the date of the list request, all information and records relating to the list under the person's possession, custody, or control had been maintained in accordance with procedures and policies that are consistent with sections 6001 and 6112 of the Internal Revenue Code; and state that the extension request is not being made to avoid the person's list maintenance obligations imposed by section 6112 and its corresponding regulations. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6708-1(g)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6708-1#p-301.6708-1(g)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6708-1(g)(1). A material advisor seeking to avoid the section 6708 penalty for failing to make a list of advisees available to the IRS must show that the failure was due to reasonable cause, and the showing should address each specific day for which the list was not made available. | The section 6708 penalty will not be imposed for any day or days for which the person shows that the failure to make a complete list available to the IRS was due to reasonable cause. … The person's showing of reasonable cause should relate to each specific day or days for which the person failed to make available the requested list. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6724-1(g)(5)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6724-1#p-301.6724-1(g)(5)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6724-1 (undue hardship exception to the section 6721(a) penalty for failing to include a correct TIN on an information return). A filer seeking the exception must obtain an IRS determination by submitting a written statement, signed under penalties of perjury, describing the undue hardship and affirmatively showing that the filer was exercising or stood ready to exercise due diligence when it occurred. | A determination of undue hardship may be established only by submitting a written statement to the IRS signed under penalties of perjury that sets forth all the facts and circumstances that make an affirmative showing that the filer could have satisfied the due diligence requirements but for the occurrence of an undue hardship. Thus, the statement must describe the undue hardship and make an affirmative showing that the filer either was in the process of exercising or stood ready to exercise due diligence when the undue hardship occurred. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.6724-1(m)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.6724-1#p-301.6724-1(m)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.6724-1(m). A filer seeking a waiver of information return penalties under sections 6721 through 6723 must submit a written statement naming the specific reasonable cause provision relied on and setting forth all the facts alleged as the basis for reasonable cause, signed under penalties of perjury. | In seeking an administrative determination that the failure was due to reasonable cause and not willful neglect, the filer must submit a written statement to the address provided with the notice proposing penalty assessment (for example, Notice 972CG) or the notice of penalty assessment (for example, CP15 or CP215), or as otherwise directed by the IRS in forms, instructions or publications. The statement must— (1) State the specific provision under which the waiver is being requested, that is, paragraph (b) or under paragraphs (c)(2) through (6) or paragraph (h) of this section; (2) Set forth all the facts alleged as the basis for reasonable cause; (3) Contain the signature of the person required to file the return; and (4) Contain a declaration that it is made under penalties of perjury. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.7425-3(b)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.7425-3#p-301.7425-3(b)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.7425-3(b)(2) (IRS consent to a nonjudicial sale free of a federal tax lien). A person seeking consent submits a written application, under penalties of perjury, containing the information required for a notice of sale and a statement of the reasons why the consent is desired. | The application shall contain the information required in the case of a notice of sale, as set forth in paragraph (d)(1) of this section, and, in addition, shall contain a statement of the reasons why the consent is desired. | <sub>Treas. Reg. § 400.4-1(d)(2)</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.7429-2(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.7429-2#p-301.7429-2(a)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.7429-2(a) (administrative review of a jeopardy or termination assessment or jeopardy levy). The taxpayer’s written request for review, filed with the IRS within the prescribed period, must state fully the reasons for the request and be supported by evidence enabling the IRS to make the redetermination. | Such request shall be in writing, shall state fully the reasons for the request, and shall be supported by such evidence as will enable the district director to make the redetermination described in section 7429(a)(3). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.7430-2(c)(3)(i)(B)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.7430-2#p-301.7430-2(c)(3)(i)(B)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.7430-2(c)(3)(i)(B) (request for reasonable administrative costs). A taxpayer claiming administrative costs must include a clear and concise statement of the reasons why it alleges the IRS position was not substantially justified, identifying any published guidance the IRS allegedly failed to follow and any appellate losses on substantially similar issues. | (B) A clear and concise statement of the reasons why the taxpayer alleges that the position of the Internal Revenue Service in the administrative proceeding was not substantially justified. For administrative proceedings commenced after July 30, 1996, if the taxpayer alleges that the Internal Revenue Service did not follow any applicable published guidance, the statement must identify all applicable published guidance that the taxpayer alleges that the Internal Revenue Service did not follow. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.7515-1](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.7515-1) | Treas. Reg. § 301.7515-1. A person asking the IRS to perform a special statistical study or compilation of return data (at the requester’s cost) should fully describe the nature of the study, give detailed table specifications, and include a general statement of how the data will be used. | The requests should describe fully the nature of the study or compilation desired, giving detailed specifications for all tables to be prepared, and should include a general statement regarding the use to be made of the data requested. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.7623-1(c)(1)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.7623-1#p-301.7623-1(c)(1)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.7623-1(c)(1). A whistleblower submitting information to the IRS who knows of supporting documents or evidence not in the whistleblower’s control should describe those documents or evidence and identify their location as well as possible. | If documents or supporting evidence are known to the whistleblower but are not in the whistleblower's control, then the whistleblower should describe the documents or supporting evidence and identify their location to the best of the whistleblower's ability. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.7701(b)-8(a)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.7701(b)-8#p-301.7701(b)-8(a)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.7701(b)-8(a)(2) (residency of aliens under the substantial presence test). An alien who excludes days of U.S. presence as an exempt teacher, trainee, student, or professional athlete, or because of a medical condition, must file a statement explaining the basis for that claim. | (2) Exempt individuals and individuals with a medical condition. An alien individual must file a statement to explain the basis of the individual's claim that he or she is able to exclude days of presence in the United States because the individual— (i) Is an exempt individual as described in § 301.7701(b)-3(b)(3) (teacher/trainee) or (b)(4) (student); (ii) Is an exempt individual described in § 301.7701 (b)-3(b)(5) (professional athlete); or (iii) Has a medical condition or problem as described in § 301.7701(b)-3(c). |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-3(e)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.9100-3#p-301.9100-3(e)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-3(e)(2). A taxpayer requesting discretionary relief for a late regulatory election must submit a detailed affidavit describing the events that led to the failure to make the election and to its discovery; if the taxpayer relied on a qualified tax professional, the affidavit must describe the professional’s engagement and responsibilities and the extent of the taxpayer’s reliance. | The taxpayer, or the individual who acts on behalf of the taxpayer with respect to tax matters, must submit a detailed affidavit describing the events that led to the failure to make a valid regulatory election and to the discovery of the failure. When the taxpayer relied on a qualified tax professional for advice, the taxpayer's affidavit must describe the engagement and responsibilities of the professional as well as the extent to which the taxpayer relied on the professional. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-3(e)(3)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.9100-3#p-301.9100-3(e)(3)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-3(e)(3). A request for section 9100 relief must also include detailed affidavits from individuals with knowledge of the events (including the return preparer and advising accountants or attorneys), each describing the individual’s engagement and responsibilities and the advice given to the taxpayer. | The taxpayer must submit detailed affidavits from the individuals having knowledge or information about the events that led to the failure to make a valid regulatory election and to the discovery of the failure. These individuals must include the taxpayer's return preparer, any individual (including an employee of the taxpayer) who made a substantial contribution to the preparation of the return, and any accountant or attorney, knowledgeable in tax matters, who advised the taxpayer with regard to the election. An affidavit must describe the engagement and responsibilities of the individual as well as the advice that the individual provided to the taxpayer. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-4T(g)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.9100-4T#p-301.9100-4T(g)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-4T(g). A taxpayer applying for the Commissioner’s consent to revoke an election made under the temporary election regulations in § 301.9100-4T must file a signed application that, among other things, explains why the applicant seeks to revoke the election. | An application for consent to revoke an election shall be signed by the applicant and filed with the service center with which the election was filed and shall— (1) Contain the name, address, and taxpayer identification number of all parties identified in connection with the election, (2) Identify the election being revoked by reference to the section of the Code or Act under which the election was made, (3) Specify the scope of the election, and (4) Explain why the applicant seeks to revoke the election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-8(d)(4)(i)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.9100-8#p-301.9100-8(d)(4)(i)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-8(d)(4)(i). A taxpayer who wants to revoke an election under section 165(l) (treating losses on deposits in insolvent financial institutions as casualty losses) must request the Secretary’s consent in writing, setting forth the pertinent facts surrounding the election and the reasons for the revocation. | If a taxpayer desires to revoke an election under section 165(l) of the Code, the taxpayer must request, in writing, the consent of the Secretary setting forth the pertinent facts surrounding the election and the reasons for requesting a revocation. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-12T(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-301.9100-12T#p-301.9100-12T(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 301.9100-12T(d) (elections under the Tax Reform Act of 1976). Unless the return or a form provides otherwise, the taxpayer makes the election by a statement attached to the return identifying the Code section, the election, the period it covers, and the taxpayer’s basis or entitlement for making it. | The statement required when making an election pursuant to this section shall indicate the section under which the election is being made and shall set forth information to identify the election, the period for which it applies, and the taxpayer's basis or entitlement for making the election. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 302.1-7(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-302.1-7#p-302.1-7(a)) | Treas. Reg. § 302.1-7(a) (claims for credit or refund of tax on property returned under the Trading with the Enemy Act). A claim must contain a detailed statement, under penalties of perjury, of all the facts relied on to support the claim. | Any such claim must contain a detailed statement under penalties of perjury of all the facts relied upon in support of the claim and should be filed with the district director of the district in which the tax was paid. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 303.1-7(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-303.1-7#p-303.1-7(a)) | Treas. Reg. § 303.1-7(a) (taxes under the Trading with the Enemy Act). A claim for refund or credit of tax paid on property returned under the Act must contain a detailed statement, under penalties of perjury, of all the facts relied upon to support the claim. | Any such claim must contain a detailed statement under the penalties of perjury of all the facts relied upon in support of the claim and should be filed with the district director for the district in which the tax was paid. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.105(b)(5)(iii)(f)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.105#p-601.105(b)(5)(iii)(f)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.105(b)(5) (requests for technical advice; section 6110 disclosure). A taxpayer whose case is referred for technical advice must accompany the request with a separate statement of the deletions it proposes from the public text of the memorandum and the statutory basis for each, or a statement that only names, addresses, and identifying numbers need be deleted. | ( f ) In order to assist the Internal Revenue Service in making the deletions, required by section 6110(c) of the Code, from the text of technical advice memoranda which are open to public inspection pursuant to section 6110(a) of the Code, there must accompany requests for such technical advice either a statement of the deletions proposed by the taxpayer and the statutory basis for each proposed deletion, or a statement that no information other than names, addresses, and taxpayer identifying numbers need be deleted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.105(b)(5)(v)(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.105#p-601.105(b)(5)(v)(a)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.105(b)(5)(v)(a) (National Office technical advice conference). The conference must be held within 21 calendar days after contact, and a taxpayer wanting more time must justify the extension in writing for approval by the Technical branch chief. | The conference will be arranged by telephone, if possible, and must be held within 21 calendar days after contact has been made. Extensions of time will be granted only if justified in writing by the taxpayer and approved by the appropriate Technical branch chief. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.105(b)(5)(v)(d)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.105#p-601.105(b)(5)(v)(d)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.105(b)(5)(v)(d). Within 21 calendar days after a National Office technical advice conference, the taxpayer must furnish a written record of any additional data, reasoning, or precedents discussed at the conference that were not previously or adequately presented in writing (extensions require written justification). | ( d ) It is the responsibility of the taxpayer to furnish to the National Office, within 21 calendar days after the conference, a written record of any additional data, line of reasoning, precedents, etc., that were proposed by the taxpayer and discussed at the conference but were not previously or adequately presented in writing. Extensions of time will be granted only if justified in writing by the taxpayer and approved by the appropriate Technical branch chief. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.106(f)(5)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.106#p-601.106(f)(5)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.106(f)(5) (Appeals Rule V). To bring an unagreed case before Appeals, the taxpayer requests Appeals consideration and, when required, files a written protest setting forth specifically the reasons for refusing to accept the examination findings, with any statement of facts declared true under penalties of perjury. | In order to bring an unagreed income, profits, estate, gift, or Chapter 41, 42, 43, or 44 tax case in prestatutory notice status, an employment or excise tax case, a penalty case, an Employee Plans and Exempt Organization case, a termination of taxable year assessment case, a jeopardy assessment case, or an offer in compromise before the Appeals office, the taxpayer or the taxpayer's representative should first request Appeals consideration and, when required, file with the district office (including the Foreign Operations District) or service center a written protest setting forth specifically the reasons for the refusal to accept the findings. If the protest includes a statement of facts upon which the taxpayer relies, such statement should be declared, to be true under the penalties of perjury. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.106(f)(9)(iii)(f)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.106#p-601.106(f)(9)(iii)(f)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.106 (Appeals; technical advice and public inspection). A request for technical advice in a case before Appeals must be accompanied by a separate statement of the deletions the taxpayer proposes under section 6110(c), or a statement that only names, addresses, and identifying numbers need be deleted. | ( f ) In order to assist the Internal Revenue Service in making the deletions required by section 6110(c) of the Code, from the text of technical advice memoranda which are open to public inspection pursuant to section 6110(a) of the Code, there must accompany requests for such technical advice either a statement of the deletions proposed by the taxpayer, or a statement that no information other than names, addresses, and taxpayer identifying numbers need be deleted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(b)(5)(iv)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(b)(5)(iv)) | Statement of Procedural Rules, 26 C.F.R. § 601.201(b) (rulings on issues not yet addressed by regulations). A taxpayer who believes a business emergency exists or that unusual hardship will result without a ruling should submit with the ruling request a separate letter setting forth the facts the IRS needs to make that determination. | (iv) In any case in which the taxpayer believes that a business emergency exists or that an unusual hardship will result from failure to obtain a ruling, he should submit with the request a separate letter setting forth the facts necessary for the Service to make a determination in this regard. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(2)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(e)(2)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(2) (instructions for requesting a ruling or determination letter). Each request must contain a complete statement of all relevant facts, including a full and precise statement of the business reasons for the transaction and a carefully detailed description of the transaction. | (2) Each request for a ruling or a determination letter must contain a complete statement of all relevant facts relating to the transaction. Such facts include names, addresses, and taxpayer identifying numbers of all interested parties; the location of the district office that has or will have audit jurisdiction over the return or report of each party; a full and precise statement of the business reasons for the transaction; and a carefully detailed description of the transaction. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 7.01(1), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 22; Rev. Proc. 2026-4, section 6.02(1), 2026-1 I.R.B. 160, at 176</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(4)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(e)(4)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(4) (instructions for requesting a ruling or determination letter). A taxpayer contending for a particular determination must explain the grounds for its contentions and cite relevant supporting authorities; even if urging no particular result, it must state its views on the tax results and supporting authorities. | (4) If the taxpayer is contending for a particular determination, he must furnish an explanation of the grounds for his contentions, together with a statement of relevant authorities in support of his views. Even though the taxpayer is urging no particular determination with regard to a proposed or prospective transaction, he must state his views as to the tax results of the proposed action and furnish a statement of relevant authorities to support such views. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 7.01(9), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 26</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(5)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(e)(5)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(5) (section 6110 deletions from rulings and determination letters). A ruling or determination letter request must be accompanied by a separate statement of the deletions proposed from the public text and the statutory basis for each, or a statement that only names, addresses, and identifying numbers need be deleted. | (5) In order to assist the Internal Revenue Service in making the deletions, required by section 6110(c) of the Code, from the text of rulings and determination letters, which are open to public inspection pursuant to section 6110(a) of the Code, there must accompany requests for such rulings or determination letters either a statement of the deletions proposed by the person requesting the ruling or determination letter and the statutory basis for each proposed deletion, or a statement that no information other than names, addresses, and taxpayer identifying numbers need be deleted. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(12)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(e)(12)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.201(e)(12) (order of processing ruling, opinion letter, and determination letter requests). A taxpayer who wants its request handled ahead of the regular order submits a separate written letter showing a clear need for expedited treatment; scheduled closing dates or stock price fluctuations are not sufficient reasons. | Requests for processing ahead of the regular order, made in writing in a separate letter submitted with the request or subsequent thereto and showing clear need for such treatment, will be given consideration as the particular circumstances warrant. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 7.02(4), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 33; Rev. Proc. 2026-4, section 6.03(3), 2026-1 I.R.B. 160, at 184–85</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(n)(1)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(n)(1)(ii)) | Statement of Procedural Rules, 26 C.F.R. § 601.201 (applications for recognition of exemption in advance of operations). An organization seeking recognition of exemption before operating must fully describe the activities in which it expects to engage, including its standards, criteria, and procedures for carrying them out, its anticipated sources of receipts, and the nature of contemplated expenditures; a mere restatement of purposes is insufficient. | A mere restatement of purposes or a statement that proposed activities will be in furtherance of such purposes will not satisfy these requirements. The organization must fully describe the activities in which it expects to engage, including the standards, criteria, procedures, or other means adopted or planned for carrying out the activities; the anticipated sources to receipts; and the nature of contemplated expenditures. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-5, section 6.07(2)(a)(i)–(ii), 2026-1 I.R.B. 258, at 279</sub> |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.201(n)(9)(iii)(f)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.201#p-601.201(n)(9)(iii)(f)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.201 (technical advice for employee plans and exempt organizations; public inspection). A request for technical advice must be accompanied by a separate statement of the deletions the plan or organization proposes under section 6110(c), or a statement that only names, addresses, and identifying numbers need be deleted. | there must accompany requests for such technical advice either a statement of the deletions proposed by the plan/organization, or a statement that no information other than names, addresses, and identifying numbers need be deleted. Such statements shall be made in a separate document. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.601(c)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.601#p-601.601(c)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.601(c) (petition to issue, amend, or repeal a rule). A person petitioning the IRS identifies the statutory or regulatory sections involved and should set forth the reasons for the requested action. | A petition for the issuance of a rule should identify the section or sections of law involved; and a petition for the amendment or repeal of a rule should set forth the section or sections of the regulations involved. The petition should also set forth the reasons for the requested action. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.702(c)(6)(ii)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.702#p-601.702(c)(6)(ii)) | Treas. Reg. § 601.702(c)(6)(ii) (expedited processing of FOIA requests to the IRS). A requester seeking expedited processing must submit a statement, certified as true and correct to the best of the requester’s knowledge and belief, explaining in detail why there is a compelling need for it. | (ii) A requester who seeks expedited processing must submit a statement, certified to be true and correct to the best of his or her knowledge and belief, explaining in detail why there is a compelling need for expedited processing. |  |
| [Treas. Reg. § 601.702(g)(5)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-26/section-601.702#p-601.702(g)(5)) | Statement of Procedural Rules, 26 C.F.R. § 601.702(g)(5). When notified that its business information is the subject of a FOIA request, a business submitter may within ten days give the IRS a detailed statement of objection to disclosure, which must specify all grounds for withholding, particularly why the information is a trade secret or privileged and confidential commercial or financial information. | Such statement shall specify all grounds for withholding any of the information, with particular attention to why the information is claimed to be trade secret or commercial or financial information that is privileged and confidential. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2009-41, section 3.03, 2009-39 I.R.B. 439, at 440](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-09-41.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2009-41, section 3.03. An eligible entity that does not qualify for simplified late entity-classification relief and instead requests a letter ruling must include either the affirmative representation in section 4.04 or an explanation of why it cannot make that representation. | Additionally, eligible entities that do not qualify for relief under this revenue procedure, because they do not satisfy all of the requirements set forth in Section 4.01, and that request a letter ruling for late entity classification relief either must include as part of their letter ruling request the affirmative representation in Section 4.04 or an explanation why the entity cannot make the affirmative representation in Section 4.04. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2011-34, section 4.02, 2011-24 I.R.B. 875, at 875–76](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-11-34.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2011-34, section 4.02 (relief for a late election under Treas. Reg. § 1.469-9(g) to treat all interests in rental real estate as a single rental activity). The taxpayer attaches the election statement to an amended return, and the statement must explain why the election was not filed on time. | The statement must contain the declaration required by § 1.469-9(g)(3), must explain the reason for the failure to file a timely election, and must include the representations required in section 4.01 of this revenue procedure. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2012-33, section 19.02, 2012-34 I.R.B. 272, at 279](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-12-33.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2012-33, section 19.02 (EFTPS Batch and Bulk Provider programs). A provider appealing its suspension must file a written protest within 30 days of the suspension letter’s effective date giving detailed reasons, with supporting documentation, for withdrawing the suspension. | The written protest must be received by the Service within 30 days of the effective date on the suspension letter. The written protest must contain detailed reasons, with supporting documentation, for withdrawal of the suspension. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2013-12, Appendix C, Part II, Schedule 3, Section IV, 2013-4 I.R.B. 313, at 413–14](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-13-12.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2013-12, Appendix C, Part II, Schedule 3 (model VCP submission schedule for SEPs and SARSEPs), Section IV (Enclosures). The plan sponsor encloses a written explanation of how and why the failures occurred, including a description of the administrative procedures in effect when they occurred. | In addition to the applicable items listed on the Procedural Requirements Checklist for Form 8950, the Plan Sponsor encloses the following with this submission: … A written explanation of how and why the failure(s) described in this submission occurred, including a description of the administrative procedures applicable to the failure(s) in effect at the time the failure(s) occurred. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2014-9, section 7.02, 2014-2 I.R.B. 281, at 288](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb14-02.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2014-9, section 7.02. An organization that receives a proposed adverse determination letter from EO Determinations on its application for tax-exempt status may appeal to the Appeals Office by submitting, within 30 days, a statement of the facts, law, and arguments supporting its position and stating whether it wants a conference. | To do this, the organization must submit a statement of the facts, law and arguments in support of its position within 30 days from the date of the adverse determination letter. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2014-47, section 3.03, 2014-35 I.R.B. 393, at 398](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-14-47.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2014-47, section 3.03. A withholding foreign partnership or trust that asks the IRS to modify its WP or WT agreement by rider must describe in detail its unique facts and circumstances, propose modifications, and analyze the feasibility of those modifications. | The WP or WT request for modification of the WP agreement or WT agreement must include: (1) a detailed description of the partnership or trust's unique facts and circumstances, (2) suggested modifications to the agreement, and (3) an analysis of the feasibility of any such suggested modifications. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2015-13, section 11.03(2)(d), 2015-5 I.R.B. 419, at 443](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-15-13.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2015-13, section 11.03(2)(d). A taxpayer that disagrees with the terms and conditions of a non-automatic accounting method change letter ruling must, within 45 days of the ruling, notify the national office in writing of the disagreement and explain the reasons. | If the taxpayer disagrees with the terms and conditions in the letter ruling, within 45 calendar days from the date of the letter ruling, the taxpayer must notify the national office, in writing to the address in the letter ruling, of the disagreement and include an explanation of the reason(s). |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2015-40, section 3.02(2), 2015-35 I.R.B. 236, at 243](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-15-40.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2015-40, section 3.02(2). Before filing a competent authority request involving a taxpayer-initiated position, the taxpayer must submit a pre-filing memorandum identifying the taxpayer, explaining the factual and legal basis of the position, and describing any procedural steps taken in the treaty country and communications with the foreign competent authority. | The pre-filing memorandum must identify the taxpayer, explain the factual and legal basis of the taxpayer-initiated position, and describe any administrative, legal, or other procedural steps undertaken in the applicable treaty country (including whether the foreign tax authority has accepted an income tax return reflecting the taxpayer-initiated position for which the taxpayer seeks competent authority assistance) and any communications with the foreign competent authority regarding the position. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2021-30, section 6.09(2), 2021-31 I.R.B. 172, at 201](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-21-30.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2021-30, section 6.09(2) (EPCRS). When a plan’s failure to make required minimum distributions is corrected under VCP or Audit CAP, the plan sponsor must request a waiver of the section 4974 excise tax, and under VCP must explain the basis for the request when the affected participant is an owner-employee or 10 percent owner (an explanation is also expected under Audit CAP). | Under VCP, the Plan Sponsor, as part of the submission, must request the waiver and, in cases where the participant subject to the excise tax is either an owner-employee as defined in § 401(c)(3) or a 10 percent owner of a corporation, the Plan Sponsor must also provide an explanation supporting the request. Under Audit CAP, the Plan Sponsor must make a specific request for waiver of the excise tax under § 4974. The Plan Sponsor should also provide an explanation supporting the request for a waiver. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2023-1, section 6.03(2), 2023-1 I.R.B. 1, at 20](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb23-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2023-1, section 6.03(2). A letter ruling request on significant issues in a corporate transaction (sections 332, 351, 355, 368, or 1036) must include, for each issue, a description of the transaction, a statement of the issue, an analysis of the authorities most closely related to the issue explaining why they do not resolve it, an explanation of why the issue is significant, and the ruling requested. | Letter ruling requests under section 6.03(2) must include the following for each significant issue: (a) A narrative description of the transaction that puts the issue in context; (b) A statement identifying the issue; (c) An analysis of the relevant law, which should set forth the authorities most closely related to the issue and explain why these authorities do not resolve the issue, and an explanation concerning why the issue is significant within the meaning of section 3.01(60) of Rev. Proc. 2023-3; and (d) The precise ruling(s) requested. |  |

If the taxpayer is unable to submit an applicable representation in the form set forth in this section 3.04 (Standard Representation), the taxpayer should submit (1) an explanation for its inability to provide the Standard Representation and (2) the rationale supporting the issuance of each relevant requested ruling in the absence of the Standard Representation. If appropriate, the taxpayer should submit (1) a modified representation that addresses the same matter, (2) an explanation of the modification, and (3) the rationale supporting the issuance of each relevant requested ruling, taking into account the modified Standard Representation. |  |

The analysis of the facts must include their bearing on the requested change in method of accounting and must specify the provisions that apply. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 10.07(1), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 62](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 10.07(1) (requesting a pre-submission conference on a letter ruling). The request for a pre-submission conference, whether written or by telephone, must identify the taxpayer and briefly explain the primary issue so it can be assigned to the appropriate branch. | The request must identify the taxpayer and briefly explain the primary issue so it can be assigned to the appropriate branch. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 10.07(3), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 63](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 10.07(3). Before a pre-submission conference on a letter ruling request is scheduled, the taxpayer generally must provide a statement of whether the issue is one on which rulings are ordinarily issued and a draft ruling request or other detailed written statement explaining the proposed transaction, issue, and legal analysis. | Generally, the taxpayer will be asked to provide a statement of whether the issue is an issue on which a letter ruling is ordinarily issued and a draft of the letter ruling request or other detailed written statement explaining the proposed transaction, issue, and legal analysis, before scheduling the pre-submission conference. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 15.07(3), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 75](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 15.07(3) (reduced user fee for substantially identical plans for administering the 95-percent requirement of § 143(d)(1) under § 25(c)(2)(B)). A taxpayer requesting approval of a subsequent, substantially identical plan must describe any differences between the previously approved plan and the subsequent plan. | The request for subsequent approvals of substantially identical plans must (1) state that a prior plan was submitted and approved and include a copy of the prior plan and approval; (2) state that the subsequent plan is substantially identical to the approved plan; and (3) describe any differences between the approved plan and the subsequent plan. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-2, section 6.03, 2026-1 I.R.B. 119, at 125](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-2, section 6.03 (actions before a pre-submission conference on a request for technical advice). Before the conference, the taxpayer and the field office must exchange and submit to the Associate office statements of the pertinent facts and issues, which should include facts in dispute, issues to be discussed, legal analysis and supporting authorities, and helpful background. | The proposed statements should include any facts in dispute, the issues that the parties intend to discuss, any legal analysis and supporting authorities, and any other background information that the parties believe would facilitate the Associate office's understanding of the issues to be discussed during the conference. Prior to the scheduled pre-submission conference, the field office and the taxpayer must submit to the Associate office their respective statements of pertinent facts and issues. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-2, section 14.02, 2026-1 I.R.B. 119, at 139](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-2, section 14.02 (requests to limit retroactive effect under section 7805(b) during an examination or Appeals consideration). A taxpayer asking that the retroactive effect of a TAM (or its later modification or revocation) be limited must make the request as a TAM request that states the relief sought and explains the reasons and arguments supporting it. (The parallel letter-ruling, determination-letter, and TE/GE procedures impose a similar requirement.) | The request must meet the general requirements of a request for technical advice. It must also: (1) state that it is being made under § 7805(b); (2) state the relief sought; (3) explain the reasons and arguments in support of the relief sought; and (4) include any documents bearing on the request. | <sub>Rev. Proc. 2026-1, section 11.11(1), 2026-1 I.R.B. 1, at 67; Rev. Proc. 2026-4, section 23.08(1), 2026-1 I.R.B. 160, at 222–23; Rev. Proc. 2026-5, section 12.04(1), 2026-1 I.R.B. 258, at 288–89</sub> |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-4, section 15.02(3), 2026-1 I.R.B. 160, at 204](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-4, section 15.02(3) (Form 6088 filed with a Form 5310 or 5300 application for a determination letter on plan termination). For a cash balance plan, the applicant must explain the methodology used to calculate employees’ accrued benefits and include an illustrative example. | For cash balance plans, explain the methodology used to calculate employees’ accrued benefits, and include an example illustrating the application of the methodology. |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-4, Appendix F, item 4, 2026-1 I.R.B. 160, at 257](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-irbs/irb26-01.pdf) | Rev. Proc. 2026-4, Appendix F (additional checklist for nonbank trustee applications), item 4. An applicant for nonbank trustee approval that relies on a safe harbor in Treas. Reg. § 1.408-2(e)(2)(i) must explain how it is satisfied; if no safe harbor is met, the applicant must discuss factors bearing on the continuity requirement such as ownership concentration, employees, locations, management, and financial condition. | 4. If the applicant satisfies a safe harbor in Treas. Reg. § 1.408-2(e)(2)(i), did you explain how it was satisfied? If the applicant does not satisfy any of the safe harbors, did you discuss factors relating to satisfying the continuity requirement such as concentration of ownership, number of employees, number of physical locations, non-owner management, and overall financial condition? |  |
| [Rev. Proc. 2026-10, section 4.02(1)(c), 2026-4 I.R.B. 394, at 397](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-26-10.pdf) | Rev. | [Instructions for Form 843, at 4](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i843.pdf) | Instructions for Form 843, line 8. Explain in detail your reasons for filing this claim or request. Show your computation for the credit, refund, or abatement. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 843, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i843.pdf) | Instructions for Form 843, Requesting Net Interest Rate of Zero on Overlapping Tax Underpayments and Overpayments, line 8, item 5. A taxpayer requesting the section 6621(d) net zero interest rate must compute the interest to be credited, refunded, or abated or, if unable to do so, explain why the computation cannot be provided. | 5. A computation, to the extent possible, of the amount of interest to be credited, refunded, or abated. If you are unable to provide a computation, provide an explanation of why you are unable to provide the computation. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 843, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i843.pdf) | Instructions for Form 843, Requesting Net Interest Rate of Zero on Overlapping Tax Underpayments and Overpayments, line 8, item 6. If the net-zero interest claim involves more than one taxpayer identification number, the taxpayer must explain why the different TINs can be treated as the same taxpayer. | If your claim involves more than one taxpayer identification number (TIN), explain why the different TINs can be treated as the same taxpayer. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 843, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i843.pdf) | Instructions for Form 843, Branded Prescription Drug Fee, line 8. | [Instructions for Form 926, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i926.pdf) | Instructions for Form 926, Part III, lines 12b–12d. A U.S. transferor of foreign branch property that answers “Yes” to lines 12b and 12c must describe the foreign branch and its property and give a detailed calculation of the transferred loss amount. | Lines 12b–d. If the answer to lines 12b and 12c is “Yes,” provide the following information. • Describe the foreign branch whose property is transferred. • Describe the property of the foreign branch, including its adjusted basis and fair market value. • Set forth a detailed calculation of the transferred loss amount. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 926, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i926.pdf) | Instructions for Form 926, Part III, Section C (transfers of intangible property). The transferor must briefly explain how it determined the arm’s length price and the income inclusion, and must provide and explain the calculation of the annual deemed payment under section 367(d). | Section C, column (d). Provide a brief explanation of how you determined the arm’s length price on the date of transfer for each intangible. Section C, column (f). If you included an amount greater than zero, provide a brief explanation of how you figured the income inclusion for the year of the transfer. Provide and explain the calculation of the annual deemed payment. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 926, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i926.pdf) | Instructions for Form 926, Part III, line 14c. A transferor that chose the 20-year inclusion period must explain how it figured the increase to the deemed payment rate, including the rate for each period of the useful life and the 20-year period. | Explain how you figured the increase to the deemed payment rate for property transferred. … The explanation should include how you figured the deemed payment rate for each period of the useful life of the intangible property and the 20-year inclusion period. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 926, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i926.pdf) | Instructions for Form 926, Part III, line 14d. A transferor that chose the 20-year inclusion period explains how it estimated the anticipated income or cost reduction from the property’s use beyond the 20-year period. | Line 14d. If the answer to the question on line 14c is “Yes,” explain how you estimated the anticipated income or cost reduction attributable to the property’s (or properties’) use beyond the 20-year period. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 941-X, at 15](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i941x.pdf) | Instructions for Form 941-X, line 12 (and parallel Form 943-X and 944-X instructions). | [Instructions for Form 941-X, at 17](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i941x.pdf) | Instructions for Form 941-X, line 13 (and parallel Forms 943-X, 944-X, and CT-1X instructions). | [Instructions for Form 941-X, at 17](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i941x.pdf) | Instructions for Form 941-X, line 15 (tax adjustments) (and parallel Form 943-X and 944-X instructions). | [Instructions for Form 941-X, at 23](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i941x.pdf) | Instructions for Form 941-X, line 42 (and parallel Form 943-X and 944-X instructions). On line 43, give us a detailed reason why any worker was reclassified and, if you used section 3509 rates on lines 19–22 for any worker reclassified as an employee, explain why section 3509 rates apply and what rates you used. | <sub>Instructions for Form 943-X, at 21; Instructions for Form 944-X, at 21</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 941-X, at 24](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i941x.pdf) | Instructions for Form 941-X, line 43 (and parallel Forms 944-X, 945-X, and CT-1X instructions). The employer must explain in detail the grounds and facts relied upon to support each correction and describe in detail each correction on line 43, attaching additional sheets if needed. | 43. Explain Your Corrections Treasury regulations require you to explain in detail the grounds and facts relied upon to support each correction. On line 43, describe in detail each correction you entered in column 4 on lines 7–17, 18b–22, 25, 26b, and 26c. Also, use line 43 to describe corrections made on lines 6, 28– 29, and 35–40. | <sub>Instructions for Form 944-X, at 21; Instructions for Form 945-X, at 6; Instructions for Form CT-1X, at 19; Instructions for Form 943-X, at 21; Instructions for Form 944-X, at 22</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 941-X, at 24](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i941x.pdf) | Instructions for Form 941-X, line 43 (and parallel Form 943-X and 944-X instructions). For corrections on lines 19–22 (section 3509 rates for reclassified workers), the employer explains why the correction was necessary and attaches any IRS notice received. | For corrections shown on lines 19–22, explain why the correction was necessary and attach any notice you received from the IRS. | <sub>Instructions for Form 943-X, at 21; Instructions for Form 944-X, at 22</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, General Instructions, Section G (amended return). An organization filing an amended return enters on Schedule O which parts and schedules were amended and describes the amendments. | Check the “Amended return” box in item B in the heading area of the form. | [Instructions for Form 990, at 8](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, General Instructions (reasonable efforts to obtain information). If after reasonable efforts the organization cannot obtain information by the extended due date and is uncertain of an answer, it may use a reasonable estimate and explain on Schedule O. | If the organization is unable to obtain this information by the extended, due date after making reasonable efforts, and isn’t certain of the answer to a particular question, it may make a reasonable estimate, where applicable, and explain on Schedule O. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 10](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part I, line 1. The organization describes its mission or its most significant activities for the year on the summary page. | Line 1. Describe the organization’s mission or its most significant activities for the year, whichever the organization wishes to highlight, on the summary page. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 11](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part III, line 1. The organization describes its mission as stated in its mission statement or as adopted by its governing body, or enters “None.” | Line 1. Describe the organization’s mission as articulated in its mission statement or as otherwise adopted by the organization’s governing body, if applicable. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 11](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part III, line 2. An organization that undertook significant new program services not described on a prior Form 990 or 990-EZ answers “Yes” and describes them on Schedule O. | Line 2. Answer “Yes” if the organization undertook any new significant program services prior to the end of the tax year that it didn’t describe in a prior year’s Form 990 or 990-EZ. Describe these items on Schedule O (Form 990). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 11](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part III, lines 4a–4c (description of program services). For each program service, the organization describes its accomplishments through specific measurements and describes the activity’s objective where output is intangible. | Description of program services. For each program service reported, include the following. • Describe program service accomplishments through specific measurements such as clients served, days of care provided, number of sessions or events held, or publications issued. • Describe the activity’s objective, for both this time period and the longer-term goal, if the output is intangible, such as in a research activity. • Give reasonable estimates for any statistical information if exact figures aren’t readily available. Indicate that this information is estimated. • Be clear, concise, and complete in the description. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 11](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part III (public interest law firms). A section 501(c)(3) or 501(c)(4) public interest law firm lists its cases and, for each, describes the matter in dispute and explains how the litigation will benefit the public generally. | Public interest law firm. A public interest law firm exempt under section 501(c)(3) or 501(c)(4) must include a list of all the cases in litigation or that have been litigated during the year. For each case: • Describe the matter in dispute, • Explain how the litigation will benefit the public generally, and • Enter the fees sought and recovered. | <sub>Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 17</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 18](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part V, line 14b. An organization that received payments for indoor tanning services but answers “No” (did not file Form 720) explains on Schedule O why it did not file Form 720. | If it answers “No” on line 14b, it should explain on Schedule O (Form 990) why it didn’t file Form 720. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 19](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 1a. If members of the governing body do not all have the same voting rights, the organization explains the material differences on Schedule O. | If members of the governing body don’t all have the same voting rights, explain material differences on Schedule O (Form 990). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 19](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 1a. If the governing body delegated broad authority to an executive or similar committee, the organization describes on Schedule O the committee’s composition, any non-board members, and the scope of its authority. | If the organization’s governing body or governing documents delegated authority to act on its behalf to an executive committee or similar committee with broad authority to act on behalf of the governing body, and the committee held such authority at any time during the organization’s tax year, describe on Schedule O (Form 990) the composition of the committee, whether any of the committee’s members aren’t on the governing body, and the scope of the committee’s authority. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 21](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 3. An organization that delegated management duties to a management company or other person answers “Yes” and, on Schedule O, identifies the manager, describes the services provided, and lists insiders compensated by the manager. | If “Yes” on Schedule O (Form 990), list the name(s) of the management company or companies or other person(s) performing management duties; describe the services they provided to the organization; list any of the organization’s current or former officers, directors, trustees, key employees, and highest compensated employees listed in Part VII, Section A, who were compensated by the management company or companies or other person(s) during the calendar year ending with or within the organization’s tax year; and list the amounts of reportable and other compensation they received from the management company or companies or other person(s) for services provided to the filing organization and related organizations during that year. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 22](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 4. An organization that made significant changes to its organizing or enabling documents or bylaws describes the changes on Schedule O rather than attaching the amended documents. | Describe significant changes on Schedule O (Form 990), but don’t attach a copy of the amendments or amended document to Form 990 (or recite the entire amended document verbatim), unless such amended documents reflect a change in the organization’s name. | <sub>Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 20</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 22](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 5. An organization that became aware of a significant diversion of its assets explains on Schedule O the nature of the diversion, amounts or property involved, corrective actions, and pertinent circumstances, without naming the persons responsible. | Line 5. Answer “Yes” if the organization became aware during the organization’s tax year of a significant diversion of its assets, whether or not the diversion occurred during the year. If “Yes,” explain the nature of the diversion, dollar amounts and/or other property involved, corrective actions taken to address the matter, and pertinent circumstances on Schedule O (Form 990), although the person or persons who diverted the assets shouldn’t be identified by name. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 22](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 7a. If persons other than the governing body had the right to elect or appoint governing body members, the organization describes on Schedule O the classes of such persons and the nature of their rights. | Line 7a. Answer “Yes” on line 7a if at any time during the organization’s tax year there were one or more persons (other than the organization’s governing body itself, acting in such capacity) that had the right to elect or appoint one or more members of the organization’s governing body, whether periodically, or as vacancies arise, or otherwise. If “Yes,” describe on Schedule O (Form 990) the class or classes of such persons and the nature of their rights. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 22](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 7b. If governance decisions were reserved to or subject to approval by members, stockholders, or other persons, the organization describes on Schedule O those persons, the decisions requiring their approval, and their voting rights. | If “Yes,” describe on Schedule O (Form 990) the class or classes of such persons, the decisions that require their approval, and the nature of their voting rights. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 23](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, lines 8a and 8b. If the organization did not contemporaneously document meetings or written actions of its governing body or committees, it explains on Schedule O its practices or policies for such documentation. | If the answer to either line 8a or 8b is “No,” explain on Schedule O (Form 990) the organization’s practices or policies, if any, regarding documentation of meetings and written actions of its governing body and committees with authority to act on its behalf. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 23](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 10b. An organization with local chapters or affiliates that lacks written policies governing them explains on Schedule O how it ensures the local units’ activities are consistent with its exempt purposes. | If “No,” explain on Schedule O (Form 990) how the organization … ensures that the local unit’s activities are consistent with the organization’s tax-exempt purposes. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 24](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 12c. If the organization answers “Yes” that it regularly and consistently monitors and enforces compliance with its conflict of interest policy, it describes on Schedule O its practices for monitoring transactions for conflicts, including who is covered, who determines whether a conflict exists, who reviews actual conflicts, and any restrictions imposed on conflicted persons. | If “Yes,” describe on Schedule O (Form 990) the organization’s practices for monitoring proposed or ongoing transactions for conflicts of interest and dealing with potential or actual conflicts, whether discovered before or after the transaction has occurred. The description should include an explanation of which persons are covered under the policy, the level at which determinations of whether a conflict exists are made, and the level at which actual conflicts are reviewed. Also explain any restrictions imposed on persons with a conflict, such as prohibiting them from participating in the governing body’s deliberations and decisions in the transaction. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 24](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, lines 15a and 15b. If the organization answers “Yes” that its process for determining compensation of its top management official or other officers and key employees included independent review, comparability data, and contemporaneous documentation, it describes that process on Schedule O and identifies the positions to which it was applied. | If the answer was “Yes” on line 15a or 15b, describe the process on Schedule O (Form 990), identify the offices or positions for which the process was used to establish compensation of the persons who served in those offices or positions, and enter the year in which this process was last undertaken for each such person. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 25](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 18. If the organization checks “Other” to indicate how it made its Forms 1023, 990, and 990-T available for public inspection, it explains on Schedule O the other method used. | If “Other” is checked, explain on Schedule O (Form 990). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 25](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VI, line 18. If the organization did not make available upon request any of its exemption application or annual returns that are subject to the public inspection requirements, it explains the failure on Schedule O. | Also explain on Schedule O (Form 990) if the organization didn’t make publicly available upon request any of Forms 1023, 1023-EZ, 1024, 1024-A, 990, or 990-T that are subject to public inspection requirements. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 30](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VII, Section A, column (E). If an organization was related to the filer for only part of the year and the filer reports only compensation paid by it during the related period, the filer explains this on Schedule O and states the period during which the organization was related. | If the related organization was related to the filing organization for only a portion of the tax year, then the filing organization may choose to report only compensation paid or accrued by the related organization during the time it was actually related. If the filing organization reports compensation on this basis, it must explain on Schedule O (Form 990) and state the period during which the related organization was related. | <sub>Instructions for Form 990, at 31; Instructions for Form 990, Schedule O, at 2</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 37](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part VIII, line 1. Losses from uncollectible pledges, refunds of prior-year contributions, and reversals of prior-year grant expenses are not netted against contributions but are reported on Part XI, line 9, with an explanation on Schedule O. | Don’t net losses from uncollectible pledges from prior years, refunds of contributions and service revenue from prior years, or reversal of grant expenses from prior years on line 1. Rather, report any such items as “Other changes in net assets or fund balances” on Part XI, line 9, and explain on Schedule O (Form 990). | <sub>Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 10</sub> |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 50](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, Part XII, line 2c. If the organization’s process for overseeing the compilation, review, or audit of its financial statements and selecting an independent accountant changed from the prior year, it describes the change on Schedule O. | If this process has changed from the prior year, describe on Schedule O (Form 990). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 82](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, appendix on group returns. When a central organization files a group return and a Yes/No question is not answered the same way for all subordinates, it checks “Yes” (or “No” for listed lines) and explains the answer in the schedule’s supplemental information section or on Schedule O. | In general, if a line requires a “Yes” or “No” answer and the answer isn’t the same for all subordinate organizations to which the line applies, then check “Yes” and explain the answer in the schedule’s supplemental information section (if applicable) or on Schedule O (Form 990). For the following lines, however, check “No” if the answer is “No” for any of the subordinates to which the line applies, and explain on Schedule O (Form 990). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 82](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, appendix on group returns, item 11 (Part VI, line 2). Relationships among officers, directors, trustees, and key employees. Describe on Schedule O (Form 990) only relationships between officers, directors, trustees, and key employees of the same subordinate organization, not relationships between officers, directors, trustees, and key employees of one subordinate and officers, directors, trustees, and key employees of another subordinate. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990, at 82](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, appendix on group returns, item 18 (Schedule A, Part I). If subordinates in a group return do not all have the same public charity status, the central organization checks the box for the largest number and explains the differing statuses in Schedule A, Part IV. | Schedule A (Form 990), Part I. Reason for public charity status. | [Instructions for Form 990, at 83](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990, appendix on group returns, item 20 (Schedule A, Parts IV through VI). | [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, General Instructions (change in accounting method). An organization that changes its accounting method and reports the section 481(a) adjustment on Part I, line 20 must explain the change and the net asset adjustment in Schedule O (Form 990). | The organization must explain in Schedule O (Form 990), Supplemental Information to Form 990 or 990-EZ, the change and net asset adjustment. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 15](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, Part I, line 8 (Other revenue). An organization reporting income from sources not covered by lines 1 through 7 must describe that income in Schedule O (Form 990). | Line 8. Other Revenue Enter the total income from all sources not covered by lines 1 through 7. … Describe this income on Schedule O (Form 990). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 17](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, Part III (program service accomplishments). Every organization must describe the accomplishments of its three largest program services, using measures of output and, where the output is intangible, describing the activity’s short- and long-term objectives. | All organizations must describe their program service accomplishments for each of their three largest program services (as measured by total expenses incurred). • Describe program service accomplishments through measurements such as clients served, days of care, number of sessions or events held, or publications issued. • Describe the activity's objective, for both this time period and the longer-term goal, if the output is intangible, such as in a research activity. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 18](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, Part III, line 31. | [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 20](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, Part V, line 33. The organization must describe in Schedule O (Form 990) any significant activities not previously reported to the IRS on Form 990 or 990-EZ. | Line 33. Change in Activities Describe in Schedule O (Form 990) any significant activities that the organization conducted prior to the end of the tax year that it hasn’t previously reported to the IRS on Form 990-EZ or 990. Also, describe significant activities that were discontinued. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 20](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, Part V, lines 35a and 35b. An organization with $1,000 or more of unrelated business gross income that did not file Form 990-T must explain in Schedule O (Form 990) why it did not file. | If the organization answered “Yes” to line 35a but answered “No” to line 35b because it didn’t file a Form 990-T for the tax year, then explain in Schedule O (Form 990) why the organization didn’t file a Form 990-T. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990-EZ, at 20](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990ez.pdf) | Instructions for Form 990-EZ, Part V, lines 35a and 35b. | [Instructions for Form 990 or 990-EZ, Schedule A, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990sa.pdf) | Instructions for Schedule A (Form 990), Part I, line 12g, column (iv). | [Instructions for Form 990 or 990-EZ, Schedule A, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990sa.pdf) | Instructions for Schedule A (Form 990), Part I, line 12g, column (vi). In this column, the organization may (but isn’t required to) provide an estimate of the fair market value of goods, other property, services, and use of facilities that is provided to or for the benefit of the supported organizations during the tax year. Describe in Part VI any such goods, other property, services, and use of facilities, whether or not an amount is reported for them in column (vi). |  |
| [Instructions for Form 990 or 990-EZ, Schedule A, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990sa.pdf) | Instructions for Schedule A (Form 990), Parts II and III (public support computation periods). | [Instructions for Form 990 or 990-EZ, Schedule A, at 9](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990sa.pdf) | Instructions for Schedule A (Form 990), Part II, lines 17a and 17b. | [Instructions for Form 990 or 990-EZ, Schedule A, at 14](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i990sa.pdf) | Instructions for Schedule A (Form 990), Part IV, Section A, line 3c. | [Instructions for Form 8868, at 3](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i8868.pdf) | Instructions for Form 8868, Part II (Form 5330 extension), line 2. | [Instructions for Form 8886, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i8886.pdf) | Instructions for Form 8886, line 7e. | [Instructions for Form 8886, at 7](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i8886.pdf) | Instructions for Form 8886, line 7e. Your description should include the relevance, if known, of any party (including but not limited to participants in the transaction) listed in line 8. Describe the economic and business reasons for the transaction and its structure. Describe market or business conditions creating the tax benefit(s) or consequence(s) and the transaction's financial reporting, if known. |  |

| [Instructions for Form 8918, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i8918.pdf) | Instructions for Form 8918, Item B (Protective disclosure). A material advisor filing a disclosure on a protective basis must explain on line 6a why it is filing on a protective basis. | Protective disclosure. Indicate if you are filing on a protective basis by checking the appropriate box. If you are uncertain if a transaction must be disclosed, check the “Yes” box and disclose the transaction in accordance with these instructions. On line 6a, you must explain why you are filing the disclosure on a protective basis. |  |
| [Instructions for Form 8918, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i8918.pdf) | Instructions for Form 8918, line 6a. A material advisor must give a concise statement of its role as a material advisor to the transaction and, if filing a protective disclosure, must explain why it believes it is not a material advisor. | Line 6a Provide a concise statement indicating your role as a material advisor to this transaction. | [Instructions for Form 8918, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i8918.pdf) | Instructions for Form 8918, line 13. A material advisor must describe all relevant facts about the reportable transaction, including the tax benefits, steps, purpose of each step, roles of the parties, and the economic and business reasons for the transaction and its structure. | Line 13 Describe all of the relevant facts about the reportable transaction including the following. 1. Tax benefits causing the transaction to be reportable. 2. Years affected by the transaction. 3. Steps of the transaction including: a. Agreements. b. Property transfers and acquisitions. c. Liability assumptions. d. Obligation fulfillment. e. Sales. f. Entity formation or dissolution. g. Other relevant events. Other relevant events may include but are not limited to tax result protection. Tax result protection includes insurance company and other third party products commonly described as tax result insurance. 4. Nature of the transaction (cash, loan, service, other). 5. Purpose of each step in accomplishing the tax benefits and consequences. 6. Where and how each party to the transaction (entered on lines 7a, 7b, and 8a and 8b) is used, including their roles. 7. The economic and business reasons for the transaction and its structure (describe market or business conditions creating the tax benefit or consequence and its financial reporting, if known). 8. How the financial instruments (entered on line 9) are used in the transaction. 9. How the Internal Revenue Code sections (entered on line 12) enable you to obtain the tax treatment. |  |

Answer “Yes” if you admit all patients in your community who can pay for themselves or through some form of third-party reimbursement (for example, private health insurance, Medicare, or Medicaid). Answer “No” if you limit admission for these individuals in any way and describe your admission policy in detail, including how and why you restrict patient admission. |  |

| [Publication 557, at 5](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Description of activities. An application for recognition of exemption must include a full narrative description of the organization’s past, present, and planned activities, including fundraising, anticipated receipts and contemplated expenditures, and the standards and procedures for carrying out its activities. | Description of activities. | [Publication 557, at 6](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Financial data. | [Publication 557, at 24](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Procedure for requesting a discretionary extension (late exemption application). | [Publication 557, at 26](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Educational Organizations and Private Schools. | [Publication 557, at 30](https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p557.pdf) | Publication 557, Literary Organizations.
